arXiv:2608.13736 §

Beyond Δm²: Absolute Mass Sensitivity in Neutrino Oscillations

Gustavo F. S. Alves et al.

The headline is conceptual: the oscillation phase, expanded beyond leading order in mᵢ/E, contains a term ∝ (mᵢ⁴−mⱼ⁴)L/E₀³ = Δmᵢⱼ²(mᵢ²+mⱼ²)L/E₀³, so oscillations do carry absolute-mass information through the mass-squared sum. The authors show this cannot be recovered from naive plane-wave prescriptions — equal-energy, equal-momentum, and various t↔L replacements disagree at NLO — and derive it properly with a wave-packet treatment that fixes each eigenstate’s mean energy/momentum from beta-decay kinematics (an effective two-body decay N_A→(N_B e)+ν̄). The production-dependent correction enters only at the per-mille level (E₀/m_A ≲ 10⁻⁴). Applied to JUNO’s 52.5 km reactor baseline, a χ² fit to ~9.5×10⁴ IBD events over six years yields sensitivity m₁ < 305 keV (1σ), 434 keV (2σ) with oscillation parameters profiled; the solar channel dominates because its broad dip lands in JUNO’s high-statistics band and survives energy smearing intact.

Why it matters: this overturns the textbook statement that oscillations measure only Δm². It is a genuinely new, mechanism-independent absolute-mass probe, sensitive to a different combination than beta decay (m_νe), 0νββ (m_ββ), or cosmology (Σmᵢ). The honest caveat is scale: at few-hundred-keV, this is ~10⁴× weaker than KATRIN (0.45 eV) and ~10⁷× weaker than cosmology (0.053 eV), and the sensitivity is limited by a degeneracy in which the NLO shift mimics a rescaling Δm₂₁²→Δm₂₁²(1+m₁²/2E₀²), broken only by the 1/E₀² energy dependence.

arXiv:2608.14341 §

Enhanced Three-Particle Contribution to Electroweak Penguin B-Meson Decays

Yong-Kang Huang et al.

Using SCET with two-step QCD→SCET_I→SCET_II matching, the authors compute for the first time the subleading-twist correction to B→{K,π}ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ from the three-particle twist-three B-meson distribution amplitude at NLO, plus new NNLO corrections to the spectator-scattering (C-type) and weak-annihilation topologies and the O(α_s) D-type spectator term. This is billed as the last missing ingredient for a complete leading-power factorization proof of the b→{s,d}ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ matrix element. The three-particle convolution integrals over the two soft momenta are shown to converge, and the new jet function J_C^(3P) is validated by a second, method-of-regions calculation. Numerically, the newly included leading-power effects shift both real and imaginary parts of the top-sector amplitude T_π⁰ by O(30%) in q²∈[1.5, 4.0] GeV², driven by cancellation among A- and B-type pieces; the largest phenomenological impact is on the direct-CP-asymmetry differences ΔA_CP(B→Pℓℓ), shifted 15–20% at large recoil, where the nontrivial strong phase of J_C^(3P) dominates.

Why it matters: this is the theory-side counterpart to the week’s C₉ story — tightening the leading-power factorization control that any short-distance NP interpretation of b→sℓℓ requires. The honest caveat is that it does not resolve the anomaly: branching fractions for B⁰→K⁰ℓℓ and B⁺→K⁺ℓℓ “remain in sharp tension” with Belle/CDF/LHCb/CMS at large recoil, and the new B⁺→π⁺ℓℓ predictions actually favor old LHCb data over the latest full-Run-1+2 result (1.1–2.5σ).

arXiv:2608.14119 §

Improved measurement of CP violation in B⁰ₛ→J/ψπ⁺π⁻ decays

LHCb collaboration et al.

LHCb performs a time-dependent, flavour-tagged amplitude analysis of ~94,000 B⁰ₛ→J/ψπ⁺π⁻ signal decays from the full Run 2 6 fb⁻¹ sample (38 fs decay-time resolution, 4.5% tagging power), extracting φₛ = −0.077 ± 0.034 ± 0.007 rad, |λ| = 0.993 ± 0.026 ± 0.007, and Γ_H = 0.610 ± 0.002 ± 0.004 ps⁻¹. The π⁺π⁻ spectrum is modelled with six resonances plus a nonresonant term — adding an f₂(1565) contribution to a small ~1.6 GeV peak relative to earlier work — with the CP-odd f₀(980) carrying 72.5% fit fraction. Combining with Run 1 gives φₛ = −0.046 ± 0.031 rad; folding in all other LHCb channels yields φₛ = −0.041 ± 0.017 rad (χ²/ndf = 35/30), consistent with the J/ψφ average and improving precision by ~15%.

Why it matters: against a week where the flavour front ran on anomalies, this is a clean null. The result sits squarely on the SM prediction φₛ^SM = −0.0376₋₀.₀₀₀₅⁺⁰·⁰⁰⁰⁶ rad, and no direct CP violation is seen (|λ| compatible with 1). The main caveat is explicit in the text: subleading penguin contributions are neglected throughout — externally constrained to a Δφₛ = 5.0 ± 4.6 mrad shift from B⁰→J/ψρ⁰ — and the production asymmetry A_P is fixed to zero (a ±2% variation is taken as systematic). Systematics remain subdominant to the 34 mrad statistical uncertainty on φₛ.

Observation of several sources of CP violation in B⁺→K⁺π⁺π⁻

LHCb collaboration et al.

Working the full 3 fb⁻¹ Run 1 sample (115,102 candidates, 88.7% purity), LHCb performs an amplitude analysis of \(B^+\to K^+\pi^+\pi^-\) with three independent treatments of the troublesome \(K\pi\) and \(\pi\pi\) S-waves (isobar/GLASS, K-matrix, and a first-of-its-kind quasi-model-independent binning of two crossing partial waves). All three agree, and six CP-violation phenomena clear observation-level significance at once. The headline first observations: quasi-two-body CPV in \(B^+\to\rho(770)^0K^+\), \(\mathcal{A}_{CP}=+0.280\pm0.022\pm0.029\) (minimum 5.9σ across S-wave models); and in \(B^+\to f_2(1270)K^+\), rejected at 8.2–9.1σ at amplitude level. First evidence appears for CPV in the elastic \(\pi\pi\!-\!\pi\pi\) rescattering S-wave below the \(4\pi\) threshold (\(\mathcal{A}^{\rm S}_{CP}=+0.665\pm0.032\pm0.053\)), in S–P interference with \(\rho(770)\) (5.4σ), in S–D interference near the \(f_2(1270)\) (≥4.4σ), and — a genuine first — in a spin-3 state, \(\rho_3(1690)^0\) (≥3.3σ).

Notably, no CPV is seen in \(\rho^0\)–\(\omega\) mixing, contrary to several predictions; this dilution explains why the fitted \(\rho(770)\) asymmetry exceeds the model-independent P-wave value (0.150±0.022). A large positive asymmetry immediately above \(\pi\pi\) threshold resists all three models, hinting at sizeable \(c\bar c\)-loop or isospin-2 contributions. Systematics are comparable to statistical uncertainties throughout — good model control, but a signal that Run 1 statistics can no longer fully exploit. This is the measurement-side counterpart to the season’s flavor anomalies, sharpening reference points for \(\gamma\) extraction and \(B\to K\pi\)-type isospin sum rules.

arXiv:2608.12463 §

Probing CP-violating top-quark dipole moments with tomographic observables

Maxim Perelstein et al.

Perelstein, Shaik and Youn recast \(t\bar t\) spin tomography as a CP-violation search. Symmetry fixes which Fano coefficients a CP-odd (chromo-)electric dipole can populate: the normal polarization difference \(\Delta B_n\) and the antisymmetric normal-plane correlations \(A_{rn}\), \(A_{kn}\), all exactly zero in the SM (three-loop suppressed). They compute the production density matrix in closed form for \(e^+e^-\), \(q\bar q\) and \(gg\), build the statistically optimal CP-odd observable \(O_{\rm opt}=f_1/f_0\) (proven to saturate the Cramér–Rao bound), and carry the analysis to Delphes detector level. Projected 95% CL reach on inverse couplings: 10–15 TeV for the electroweak electric dipoles \(d_\gamma,d_Z\) at FCC-ee (365 GeV, 3 ab⁻¹), and 40 (200) TeV for the top chromo-EDM at LHC Run 2 (HL-LHC) — exceeding the conventional triple/quadruple-product observables (e.g. the CMS \(O_1\) search) used today. \(d_\gamma\) projects mainly onto \(A_{rn}\), \(d_Z\) onto \(\Delta B_n\); the optimal weight combines them for ~40 TeV parton-level FCC-ee reach.

A sharp negative result accompanies this: they prove a no-go theorem that the top–antitop discord asymmetry \(\Delta\mathcal{D}\) (and geometric discord, steering asymmetry) vanishes identically for the dipoles, contradicting a recent claim that advertised it as a CP probe — the reported nonzero values are CP-even absorptive artifacts. Caveats: reach at FCC-ee threshold is diluted by poor top-direction reconstruction (\(\sim40^\circ\), dilution factors 0.24–0.40); systematics are assumed subdominant, and the EFT-consistent linear-order truncation neglects dimension-eight-degenerate terms.

arXiv:2608.12793 §

High-precision measurement of the space-like η' transition form factor

BESIII Collaboration et al.

BESIII measures the single-virtual space-like \(\eta'\) TFF via single-tag two-photon fusion \(e^+e^-\to e^+e^-\eta'\) on 20.3 fb⁻¹ at 3.773 GeV, reconstructing \(\eta'\to\pi^+\pi^-\gamma\) and \(\pi^+\pi^-\eta\). The result spans \(Q^2\in[0.1,6.0]\) GeV² in 18 fine bins, reaches better than 3% precision below 1.5 GeV², and provides the first direct determination at \(Q^2<0.3\) GeV² — filling the gap between BaBar (>4 GeV²) and L3 (<0.15 GeV², which inferred \(Q^2\) indirectly from the \(\eta'\) transverse momentum). Tight control of the untagged-photon virtuality (\(Q^2_{\rm miss}\in[0,0.08]\) GeV², with a +1.0% correction to \(Q^2_{\rm miss}=0\)) is the key improvement; total systematics run 2.5–4.7% on the cross section, halved on the TFF.

The extracted values are the most precise to date and lie systematically above earlier data — attributed to the larger, less-controlled \(Q^2_{\rm miss}\) of prior measurements. Crucially, the data agree with lattice QCD (BMW) and holographic (LMR) predictions while sitting above data-driven dispersive models. Since LQCD and holographic approaches also predict a larger \(a_\mu^{\eta'}\) than the data-driven evaluation, this input feeds directly into the hadronic light-by-light contribution to muon \(g-2\) — where LQCD HLbL now runs ~50% above the 2020 white-paper number — and the authors flag that existing data-driven analyses may warrant re-evaluation. The result also constrains the \(\eta'\) gluonic component and \(\eta'\) production models.

arXiv:2608.12215 §

Model-independent measurement of the transversity amplitudes of the B⁰→K^*0μ⁺μ⁻ decay

LHCb collaboration et al.

LHCb reconstructs the full set of \(B^0\to K^{*0}\mu^+\mu^-\) transversity amplitudes unbinned in \(q^2\) over \(1.1<q^2<8.0\,\mathrm{GeV}^2/c^4\), expanding each P-wave amplitude in four Legendre polynomials (32 parameters, S-wave as nuisance) fitted to 8.4 fb⁻¹. The orthogonality of the Legendre basis reduces amplitude correlations relative to earlier empirical parametrisations, and CP-averaged observables (\(S_5\), \(A_{\rm FB}\), etc.) are then built as continuous functions of \(q^2\). Systematics run 0.09–0.35 of the statistical error, dominated by kinematic/trigger corrections. Profiling in flavio with the Ref. [14] baseline binning gives \(\mathrm{Re}(C_9^{\rm NP})=-1.17^{+0.21}_{-0.20}\), a \(4.3\sigma\) pull; quartering the bins tightens to \(-1.17^{+0.19}_{-0.18}\) and \(4.8\sigma\).

The key point is methodological: because hadronic (long-distance) contributions and a genuine NP \(C_9\) shift have distinct \(q^2\) dependences, the unbinned amplitude reconstruction lets global fits choose their own binning and, in principle, disentangle the two. The \(C_9\) deviation surviving this more flexible treatment — and growing with finer \(q^2\) resolution — is exactly what one expects from a real short-distance effect rather than a mismodelled charm-loop contamination. Results agree with the latest binned angular analysis [14]. Caveat: the interpretation still fixes all other Wilson coefficients to SM values and imports SM hadronic nuisance parameters through flavio’s covariance; the significance is a one-parameter statement, and the amplitude fit assumes no scalar/timelike contributions and a uniform-in-\(q^2\) S-wave. The disentangling of hadronic versus NP awaits improved theory predictions to compare against the measured amplitudes.

Anomalies in Hadronic B Decays

Marianne Bouchard et al.

This BEACH 2026 proceedings summarises SU(3)\(_F\) global fits to \(B\to PP\) (\(P\in\{\pi,K,\eta,\eta'\}\)) in the topological-diagram basis, using EWP-tree relations to reduce 10 complex amplitudes to seven effective diagrams (13 real parameters). Individually the \(\Delta S=0\) (\(\chi^2/{\rm dof}=1.1/2\), \(p=58\%\)) and \(\Delta S=1\) (\(1.6/2\), \(p=45\%\)) fits are excellent, but the combined SU(3)\(_F\)-symmetric fit collapses to \(\chi^2/{\rm dof}=43/17\), a \(3.6\sigma\) SM discrepancy. Adding \(\eta,\eta'\) (25 parameters) worsens the fit to \(4.1\sigma\); the \(B\to VV\) extension exceeds \(7\sigma\).

Imposing theory-motivated constraints makes things worse, not better: forcing \(|\tilde C/\tilde T|=0.2\) (QCD-factorisation colour-counting) against the fitted \(0.85\pm0.12\) pushes to \(4.9\sigma\), and setting the annihilation diagram \(\tilde A=0\) (expected \(\sim f_B/m_B\)-suppressed, yet fitted \(\sim|\tilde T|\)) gives \(5.2\sigma\). The claim to near-rigour rests on the EWP-tree relations being modified only ~10% by neglected \(c_{7,8}\). This is the day’s second, independent \(B\)-anomaly hint, from the non-leptonic sector. Caveat: it is a proceedings, results drawn from Refs. [6–9]; the central assumption is exact SU(3)\(_F\) with a fixed \(\theta_\eta=19.5°\), and the authors themselves flag that whether a genuine ~30% breaking could accommodate the data “has to be checked” — work in progress.

arXiv:2608.12293 §

Measurement of the ν̅_μ–Hydrogen CCQE cross section using the NOvA Near Detector

The NOvA Collaboration

NOvA isolates \(\bar\nu_\mu\mathrm{H}\to\mu^+n\) from the 10.8%-hydrogen scintillator/PVC near detector (1.2×10²¹ POT), exploiting the nuclear-effect-free kinematics of a free-proton target: two BDT discriminants (topological HitID, kinematic KineID) plus data-driven control regions yield 35,509 background-subtracted signal events — the highest-statistics (anti)neutrino–hydrogen sample ever. This lands squarely on the digest’s theme: the same cross-section systematics that were shifting DUNE sensitivities 25–30% (Puhan et al.) are precisely what a nuclear-effect-free reference process pins down. The data-correction scheme is validated by 10,000 fake-data throws showing background predictions stable against ~60% MC variations, with \(k_{\rm cut}\) factors anticorrelated with the model shifts. Caveats are candidly stated: QEH purities are only ~41%/36% and signal efficiencies 3.6%/4.8%, so the analysis leans heavily on control-region-driven background subtraction; FSI weights are derived solely from neutrino-mode inclusive CC data and applied unchanged to antineutrino mode. The flux uncertainty still dominates the cross section itself — the payoff is prospective, once this becomes a flux standard candle.

An improved direct limit on the muon electric dipole moment

2 Collaboration et al.

The Fermilab Muon g−2 collaboration sets \(d_{\mu^+}=(-0.35\pm0.19_{\rm stat}\pm0.34_{\rm sys})\times10^{-19}\,e\,\)cm from its 2019–20 straw-tracker data (\(2.34\times10^9\) positrons across Run-2, Run-3a, Run-3b), yielding \(|d_\mu|<1.10\times10^{-19}\,e\,\)cm at 95% CL — a factor 1.5 improvement on the BNL E821 limit. The measurement uses the traceback method: a muon EDM tilts the spin-precession plane, producing an oscillation in the average vertical decay angle \(\langle\theta_y\rangle\) that is \(\pi/2\) out of phase with the anomalous precession. Data are fit in 8 momentum bins × 2 stations × 3 runs (48 measurements, average reduced \(\chi^2=1.08\)), combined via BLUE. Combining with BNL tightens the bound to \(|d_\mu|<0.98(0.86)\times10^{-19}\,e\,\)cm at 95(90)% CL.

Why it matters: the result stays ~19 orders of magnitude above the SM prediction (\(\sim10^{-38}\,e\,\)cm), so it functions as a clean BSM null test on a CP-violating source independent of the anomalous-moment program, complementing the model-dependent indirect bound (\(1.9\times10^{-20}\)).

Caveat, honestly flagged: this is systematics-limited, dominated (by far) by the vertical-alignment false-signal correction (\(3.12\times10^{-20}\), vs. \(1.87\) statistical). Notably, the analysis failed the initial soft-unblinding \(3\sigma\) check, triggering the internal review that uncovered the vertical-misalignment and longitudinal-field systematics; a data-driven correction was then developed while still blinded. The alignment uncertainty is itself statistics-limited and should shrink with Runs 4–6 (2.5× the present dataset).

arXiv:2608.10961 §

TauPolaris: reconstructing tau lepton polarimetric vectors with conditional normalizing flows

Daniel Winterbottom et al.

TauPolaris estimates the undetected neutrino momenta in \(\tau\) decays with a conditional normalizing flow (nine spline-coupling blocks, transformer conditioning network), modeling the full joint density \(p(\bm\nu\mid\mathbf{c})\) rather than a point regression. This preserves the correlations between momentum components — crucially reconstructing the \(\tau\) mass at the physical 1.78 GeV, where an MSE-trained transformer biases low and pushes the neutrino collinear with the visible \(\tau\). A gradient-ascent MAP estimate in the flow’s latent space gives per-event neutrino configurations with ~40% better angular resolution than the transformer and ~20% better than sampling. On simulated LHC \(pp\) collisions with Delphes+CMS-HPS detector modeling, three applications are demonstrated: distinguishing quantum entanglement from a classically-correlated null in \(H\to\tau\tau\) at \(>4.3\sigma\) (and Bell witness \(m_{12}\) at \(4.0\sigma\)) with 3 ab⁻¹; improving CP-violation sensitivity via \(\phi_{CP}\) by 18% overall (up to 88% in \(3\pi^\pm0\pi^0\)–\(3\pi^\pm0\pi^0\), and enabling \(3\pi^\pm1\pi^0\) reconstruction for the first time); and new polarimetric variables to suppress \(Z\to\tau\tau\).

Why it matters: this moves the collider-QI program from top-quark tomography toward a deployable analysis tool, extending the optimal polarimetric-vector method to every \(\tau\) decay topology (previously restricted to 3-prong modes with secondary vertices).

Caveats: entirely simulation-based, with hand-added track smearing to patch Delphes; the entanglement \(4.3\sigma\) is an upper limit set by finite pseudoexperiments, and yields are extrapolated from a single BDT window assuming preserved S/B. The \(\mathcal{C}\)/\(m_{12}\) estimators are biased on finite data, requiring pseudoexperiment calibration.

arXiv:2608.10064 §

Electroweak precision observables at NLL order in the SMEFT

Ulrich A. Haisch et al.

Haisch and Stefanek upgrade SMEFT predictions for electroweak precision observables to next-to-leading-logarithmic accuracy by combining one-loop EW matching with two- and one-loop RG evolution from \(\Lambda\) to \(m_Z\), leveraging the newly available two-loop SMEFT beta functions. A key methodological point, proven via the \(T\)-parameter contribution of the right-handed four-top operator \(Q_{uu}^{3333}\): two-loop matching is not needed for NLL — higher-order matching cannot generate \(\ln(\Lambda/m_Z)\) logarithms, so one-loop matching plus NLL running suffices. The headline deliverable is numerical NLL expressions encoding the full dependence on all 210 independent Wilson coefficients (excluding only \(Q_H\), which enters at NNLL), released as ancillary files, with explicit results tabulated for five benchmark operators.

Why it matters: whereas tree-level matching reaches only 23 operators, NLL running opens EWPO sensitivity to the entire operator set — including operators contributing only at two-loop order (\(Q_G\), \(Q_{HG}\), \(Q_{uH}^{33}\)), which have no one-loop EWPO effect at all. For the top chromomagnetic dipole \(Q_{uG}^{33}\), the genuine two-loop \(L\) (green) terms actually dominate the \(L^2\)/\(L\) (red) terms, driven by a large \(y_t^3\) beta function mixing into \(Q_{HD}\). This is positioned as the reference basis for FCC-ee/CEPC global fits at sub-permille precision, continuing the SMEFT-precision thread.

Caveats: the framework is illustrated on current LEP/SLC fits and projections rather than delivering a full global result here; couplings are fixed at \(m_Z\) (differences are NNLL). The authors note complementary concurrent work (Mantani et al.; Born et al.), and stress that a complete analysis must retain both oblique and non-oblique observables.

arXiv:2608.07657 §

Neutrinoless Double-Beta Decays from Operator Mixing

J. de Vries et al.

(abstract only) A complete one-loop SMEFT renormalization-group analysis shows that dimension-seven operators which do not contribute to \(0\nu\beta\beta\) at tree level nonetheless generate sizable contributions via operator mixing under RG running. Accounting for the full one-loop evolution, the resulting \(0\nu\beta\beta\) constraints become the most stringent bounds to date on numerous dim-7 operators — improving on limits from meson decays. The authors stress that effects beyond the first leading logarithm can dominate for specific operators and flavor combinations.

Why it matters: this is the day’s most experiment-adjacent rigor, converting the extreme sensitivity of \(0\nu\beta\beta\) into a systematic constraint on the broader lepton-number-violating operator basis rather than a single-parameter mass bound. Caveat: only the abstract is available, so the numerical bounds and the flavor/operator specifics cannot be verified here.

arXiv:2608.08394 §

The π/12 model: trimaximal first-column lepton mixing with charged-lepton μ–τ breaking

Vernon Barger

Barger decomposes the first PMNS column into two independent conditions — the trimaximal norm \(|U_{e1}|^2=2/3\) (\(\varepsilon_1\)) and the \(\mu\)–\(\tau\) balance \(|U_{\mu1}|=|U_{\tau1}|\) (\(\varepsilon_2\)) — and shows that including the first JUNO result the data treat them asymmetrically: \(\varepsilon_1=+0.014\pm0.010\) (consistent with zero at \(1.4\sigma\)) while \(\varepsilon_2=+0.29^{+0.07}_{-0.13}\), large in central value but disfavoring zero only at \(1.5\sigma\) because the profile likelihood in \(\delta\) is shallow. A TM\(_1\) neutrino sector with the electron-row condition \(|U_{e2}|/|U_{e3}|=2+\sqrt3\) fixes \(\sin^2\theta_{13}=(2-\sqrt3)/12=0.02233\) and \(\sin^2\theta_{12}=0.31811\); a single charged-lepton 2–3 rotation (angle \(\theta_e\simeq-1.9^\circ\), phase forced to \(\alpha=0\) by flavon alignment in an \(S_4\times C_4\times C_3\times C_2\) realization) generates \(\varepsilon_2=\sin2\theta_e\cos\alpha\) and pins \(\delta=272^\circ\pm2^\circ\), with \(\varepsilon_2=-0.066\) — opposite in sign to the measured central value.

Why it matters: the framework is unusually falsifiable. The predicted \(\delta\) sits \(1.7\sigma\) from the current central value on the present profile likelihood, and Hyper-Kamiokande’s \(20^\circ\) design precision resolves it at \(3\sigma\); a confirmed second octant (\(\delta\approx177^\circ\)) would exclude it at \(>4\sigma\). Caveat: the rigid mass sum \(\Sigma m_\nu=65.6\) meV sits at/just beyond the DESI \(\Lambda\)CDM bound (64.2 meV), requiring dynamical dark energy if cosmology tightens; \(m_{\beta\beta}=5.4\) meV is below current \(0\nu\beta\beta\) reach.

arXiv:2608.08803 §

The Challenge of Detecting Quantum Nature of Gravitational Waves

Yu Miyauchi et al.

Working with Gaussian states and a linear single-mode detector coupling, Miyauchi et al. use a squeezing witness \(W=|M|-N\) (\(W>0\) certifies sub-vacuum quadrature variance impossible for any coherent-state mixture) to track whether source squeezing survives projection onto the detector-accessible wave-packet mode. Three source scenarios all fail: inflationary two-mode squeezing reduces to a thermal marginal (\(W_A=-N_A<0\)); a single-mode-squeezed stochastic background has random phases across coherence patches, washing out \(|M_A|\); an isolated source is suppressed geometrically by \(W_A\le\Delta\Omega(\ell_{\rm eff}+1)^2/8\pi\), giving \(\Delta\Omega\sim(3\,{\rm km}/100\,{\rm Mpc})^2\sim10^{-42}\) for ground detectors unless emission is collimated to \(\ell_{\rm eff}\gtrsim10^{21}\).

They then show source squeezing is inessential: a pre-squeezed detector state acquires a positive witness \(W^{(Q)}_{\tilde b}<\sin^2\epsilon/2\) from a quantized wave even when the incident mode is unexcited, whereas a classical \(c\)-number field only displaces the detector (\(W^{(C)}_{\tilde b}=0\)). But the witness is throttled by the graviton–detector coupling \(\bar\epsilon=h_{\rm QG}/h_{\rm th}\), which their strain-sensitivity table gives as \(\sim10^{-17}\)–\(10^{-33}\) across nHz–THz; detection needs \(\epsilon\gtrsim N_{\rm trial}^{-1/4}\), and coherent integration (\(\epsilon\propto\sqrt{\omega T}\)) reaches only \(\sim10^{-8.5}\) for a THz signal over a year.

Why it matters: this is the second no-go in two weeks, explicitly extending the Bao et al. (2607.27313) coarse-graining/weak-transfer argument to gravitational waves. The detector-squeezing loophole is real but doesn’t defeat the coupling suppression — the program’s wall is confirmed from another direction. Caveat: results are restricted to Gaussian states and linear single-mode coupling, which the authors argue is the optimistic case.

arXiv:2608.07332 §

Combination of Higgs boson measurements at √s = 13 TeV and their interpretations by the ATLAS experiment

ATLAS Collaboration

(Abstract only available.) ATLAS combines its full Run 2 (up to 140 fb⁻¹) Higgs measurements into a single reference, reporting an inclusive signal strength of \(0.990^{+0.053}_{-0.051}\) — split as \(\pm0.027\) (stat.), \(\pm0.024\) (exp.), \(^{+0.037}_{-0.035}\) (sig. theo.), \(^{+0.016}_{-0.015}\) (bkg. theo.). The signal-theory term, dominated by missing higher-order QCD and parton-shower modelling, is now the leading uncertainty, so precision is theory-limited rather than statistics-limited. Sensitivities improve up to 35% over the previous combination. Combining single-Higgs and \(HH\) rates gives \(\kappa_\lambda=1.3^{+3.1}_{-1.6}\) (\([-1.5,6.5]\) at 95% CL); the on-/off-shell rates yield \(\Gamma_H=3.6^{+2.1}_{-1.6}\) MeV; and the \(H\to b\bar b\) partial width delivers a first ATLAS running \(b\)-mass at the Higgs scale, \(m_b(m_H)=2.73^{+0.27}_{-0.25}\) GeV (MSbar). Kinematics are probed via STXS in 44 regions and interpreted in SMEFT.

Why it matters: this is the document every future coupling, self-coupling, and width comparison will cite, closing out the incremental \(HH\)/\(tH\) updates the memory has tracked. The signal strength sitting on unity at percent-level with theory now dominant sharpens where the community must invest — better fixed-order and shower predictions rather than more luminosity. The \(b\)-mass extraction demonstrates the Higgs as a running-mass probe. Caveats are structural: \(\kappa_\lambda\) and \(\Gamma_H\) remain broad, and the width relies on the on-/off-shell interpretation with its model assumptions.

arXiv:2608.06480 §

Cosmic birefringence from a joint analysis of ACT and Planck

Johannes R. Eskilt

Eskilt performs the first joint isotropic-birefringence fit to ACT DR6 and Planck DR4 polarization, exploiting cross-power spectra between the two experiments — which break the \(\alpha+\beta\) miscalibration degeneracy in independent ways (Planck via Galactic foreground \(EB\), ACT via optics-model priors on \(\alpha\), probing only \(\ell>600\)). Sampling 17 miscalibration angles, three dust-\(EB\) amplitudes, and one \(\beta\), the baseline gives \(\beta=0.277^\circ\pm0.057^\circ\), excluding zero at \(4.8\sigma\). Crucially, positive \(EB\) appears in all three blocks separately: ACT×ACT (\(0.207^\circ\pm0.073^\circ\)), ACT×Planck (\(0.178^\circ\pm0.078^\circ\), \(2.3\sigma\)), and Planck×Planck (\(0.387^\circ\pm0.094^\circ\)). A conservative dust-mitigation test — masking Planck to 30% Galactic cut, keeping only \(\ell>600\), dropping foreground-dominated bands — still yields \(0.236^\circ\pm0.067^\circ\) at \(3.5\sigma\), so recovering \(\beta=0\) requires both the \(\alpha\) priors and the dust-\(EB\) model to fail simultaneously.

Why it matters: a frequency-independent \(\beta\neq0\) is the signature of an axion-like field coupled to electromagnetism via a Chern-Simons term — parity violation beyond ΛCDM, a direct window on the dark sector. Two independent hints now cross-correlate into the strongest signal yet. Caveats are candidly stated: the ACT×ACT block fits poorly (PTE 0.1%, an unexplained PA5 f090/f150 angle discrepancy the ACT team flagged), there is \(1.5\sigma\) Planck–ACT tension in preferred \(\beta\), the HFI priors are pre-flight, and the author’s pipeline underestimates ACT \(EB\) errors by ~7%. Unresolved systematics preclude a firm cosmological conclusion.

arXiv:2608.06731 §

Laboratory-frame T-matrix and heavy quark drag in the quark-gluon plasma

Anurag Tiwari et al.

Tiwari and He solve the in-medium two-body \(T\)-matrix directly in the medium rest (laboratory) frame rather than the pair center-of-mass frame, where the standard partial-wave reduction assumes the total pair-momentum \(\vec P\) dependence is negligible. Since a thermal medium breaks Lorentz invariance and defines a preferred frame, physical observables must be built from lab-frame amplitudes. Exploiting the residual azimuthal symmetry about the \(\vec P\)-axis, they decompose the five-dimensional scattering equation into uncoupled integral equations for azimuthal components \(T_m\), making it numerically tractable. For charm–light-quark scattering (\(m_Q=1.3\) GeV, \(m_q=0.4\) GeV, \(T=190\) MeV, screened Cornell potential), the lab- and CM-frame amplitudes coincide at \(P=0\) but diverge sharply as \(P\) grows: in non-forward regions \(|T_{\rm CM}|^2\) overshoots by 40–150% at \(P=1\)–1.5 GeV, and the relativistic normalization \(R_E\) further distorts the forward peaks. Folded into the drag coefficient, the lab-frame result exceeds the CM baseline by 25–30% at \(T=190\) MeV (low \(p_Q\)) and 30–40% at \(T=258\) MeV across most momenta.

Why it matters: heavy-quark drag is the key transport parameter extracted from charm-hadron flow to characterize the near-ideal QGP. A previously unquantified 25–40% frame-mismatch bias directly reduces the theory uncertainty in inferring QGP transport properties — a concrete tightening. Caveats: the calculation uses constant quark widths (\(\Gamma=100\)–200 MeV, itself a 20–30% spread) rather than a self-consistent spectral function, treats only heavy–light-quark (not quark–gluon) scattering, and has not yet been propagated through Langevin transport to observables.

arXiv:2608.05269 §

Baryogenesis via the CKM Matrix with Minimal Flavor Violation

Innes Bigaran et al.

Bigaran, Krnjaic, Langhoff and Xiao construct an explicit counterexample to the folklore that SM CP violation is too small for baryogenesis. The model adds a scalar leptoquark \(\mathcal{S}\sim(\bar{\mathbf 3},\mathbf 1)_{1/3}\times\mathbf 3_{\bar d}\) whose baryon- and lepton-violating couplings are built entirely from Yukawa spurions under MFV, so the only CP phase is the Jarlskog invariant \(J\). Flavor breaking splits the three \(\mathcal{S}_i\) masses (\(m_i^2=M^2(1+\gamma y_{d_i}^2)\)), which lets tree–propagator interference in \(\mathcal{S}_i\) decays generate a CP asymmetry per decay that peaks near resonance at \(\epsilon_1^{\max}\approx 0.15\) — vastly above the naive \(Y_B\sim 10^{-20}\) estimate. Crucially, no time-varying parameters are needed, distinguishing it from Mesogenesis. Two cosmologies are offered: low reheat (\(T_{\rm RH}<130\) GeV) with non-thermal \(\mathcal{S}\) production, requiring \(m_{\mathcal S}\lesssim10^{11}\) GeV; or high reheat with right-handed-neutrino \(\Delta L=2\) washout converting \(B{+}L\) into surviving \(B{-}L\), viable for \(\epsilon_i\gtrsim10^{-7}\).

Why it matters: it directly challenges the “SM CP violation is insufficient” verdict that motivates much BSM baryogenesis, and anchors the digest’s flavor/CP top-down thread. The honest caveats are prominent: the vertex-correction contribution vanishes and the entire asymmetry rides on the propagator term, which dies at mass degeneracy; the \(B{-}L\)-preserving decays require an extra ingredient (a late-decaying scalar or RH neutrinos) to escape sphaleron washout; and proton decay forces \(M\gtrsim10^{14}\) GeV for electron couplings (relaxed to \(\sim6\times10^9\) GeV for \(\tau\)). The authors explicitly frame it as a proof of principle, not a classified model.

arXiv:2608.05330 §

Photon-nucleon entanglement in Compton scattering at low and high energies

Yoshitaka Hatta et al.

Hatta and Martínez-Fernández extend the collider quantum-information program to elastic Compton scattering, treating the final photon helicity and nucleon spin as two qubits. Their central analytic result is a no-go theorem: in unpolarized scattering off any spin-\(\tfrac12\) target with real amplitudes, the partially-transposed density matrix cannot have a negative eigenvalue, so no entanglement is possible — proven generally, extending an earlier numerical electron-only observation. To generate entanglement they turn to polarized initial states. Below the pion threshold (\(\omega<140\) MeV) all six helicity amplitudes are real, yet with polarized beams they find a rich pattern of maximally entangled Bell states (and unitary equivalents, including a cyclic-anticorrelation \(\Psi^{\circlearrowright}\)) distributed across the \((\theta,\omega)\) plane, with proton and neutron showing distinct structure. They also compute the high-energy wide-angle regime at NLO in pQCD via GPD-based soft form factors, where an \(\mathcal{O}(\alpha_s)\) imaginary part gives entanglement everywhere but never maximal (\(\lambda_{\min}\approx-0.36\) at best).

The striking physics point is diagnostic: for the neutron, where the Thomson term is absent, the electric and magnetic polarizabilities \(\alpha_E^n,\beta_M^n\) control not just the pattern but the very existence of entanglement — setting \(\alpha_E^n=\beta_M^n=0\) makes the \((\hat x,\hat x)\) configuration separable. This positions entanglement as a probe of nucleon EM structure, measurable at MAMI, JLab and a future EIC. Caveat: measuring final-state spin correlations is acknowledged as experimentally challenging, and the free-neutron extraction relies on deuteron subtraction, left to future work.

arXiv:2608.05708 §

Three-baryon femtoscopy as an effective 3→3 scattering experiment

ALICE Collaboration

ALICE measures the three-proton correlation function in 13.6 TeV pp collisions (Run 3, 53.1 pb\(^{-1}\), with dedicated software triggers selecting triplets at \(Q_3<1.4\) GeV/\(c\), found once per 30000 minimum-bias events). A data-driven decomposition isolates the genuine p–p–p signal (79% of triplets) from \(\Lambda\)/\(\Sigma^+\) feed-down and misidentification backgrounds, with the source size \(\rho_0=(2.54\pm0.04)\) fm fixed by the companion two-proton measurement (\(r_0=1.27\) fm). Comparison to hyperspherical-adiabatic three-body continuum calculations (AV18 + Coulomb, three-body force negligible by Pauli blocking) reproduces the data at \(n_\sigma=2.5\). The headline sensitivity result: removing the NN \(p\)-wave interaction produces a \(22.2\sigma\) disagreement, proving the correlation function resolves the partial-wave structure of the nuclear force. Two novel continuum signatures emerge — the nuclear interaction stays relevant up to grand-angular momentum \(K\le7\) (far slower convergence than the two-body case, since large \(K\) still admits pairs in \(l_x=0\)), and the correlation stays above unity as it approaches the uncorrelated limit, the first experimental signature of an effective long-range attraction (\(\propto\zeta/\rho^3\), with \(\zeta\) tied to the two-body scattering length).

This establishes femtoscopy as a genuine 3→3 scattering experiment and gives the first direct access to the isospin-3/2 three-body continuum, previously reachable only in isospin-1/2 via nucleon–deuteron scattering. Caveats acknowledged by the collaboration: the three-body Coulomb problem is treated approximately (no exact solution), feed-down modeling could be sharpened, and the \(2.5\sigma\) agreement leaves room for theory improvement. The clear payoff is extension to p–p–\(\Lambda\), p–p–\(\Sigma\), p–p–\(\Xi\) for the strangeness sector.

arXiv:2608.04059 §

A Bayesian approach to the long-baseline neutrino oscillation sensitivity of DUNE

DUNE Collaboration et al.

DUNE recasts its established sensitivity inputs (the same flux, GENIE-based cross-section, and detector models as the 2020 TDR) into a Bayesian MCMC framework using MaCh3, with Adaptive Metropolis proposal tuning (~50× fewer steps than manual tuning, further reduced ~15× by 5% downsampling). This is explicitly not an updated sensitivity but a demonstration of what the Bayesian treatment adds. Two payoffs stand out. First, four-dimensional posteriors expose the strong sin²θ₂₃–sin²θ₁₃ correlation, producing two well-separated octant lobes; applying a reactor short-baseline θ₁₃ constraint post hoc via prior reweighting yields “a significant increase” in θ₂₃-octant sensitivity — the reactor prior breaks the appearance-channel degeneracy that otherwise smears the octant. Second, and the headline, this is the first study of DUNE’s sensitivity to the Jarlskog invariant J, a parametrization-independent CP measure derived trivially as a posterior of the sampled parameters. Studies span NuFIT 6.0 (NO, lower octant) and T2K-NOvA (IO, upper octant) Asimov points, δ_CP = 0, −π/4, −π/2, at 200 and 1000 kt-MW-yr.

Why it matters: it establishes the machinery for parametrization-independent CP inference and flexible constraint incorporation as DUNE moves toward real data, where robust systematic propagation and derived-quantity posteriors become essential. Caveats are honestly flagged: results are Asimov-based, mass-ordering sensitivity is not evaluated (assumed >5σ), θ₁₂ and Δm²₂₁ are fixed by Gaussian priors (no solar samples yet), and the ND is found to be systematics-limited with the current CC-inclusive binning.

arXiv:2608.04091 §

Moving a Detector to Probe New Neutrino Interactions: IWCD at Hyper-Kamiokande

Thomas Schwemberger et al.

Schwemberger and Takhistov reframe the movable IWCD not as a systematics-control tool but as an interaction spectrometer: sliding the same 300-ton water Cherenkov detector through the J-PARC off-axis beam (1°, 2.5°, 4°, peaking at ~1.0, 0.6, 0.4 GeV) exposes one target/apparatus to distinct incident spectra while keeping detector and interaction uncertainties correlated. Using the NCQE/CCQE event-rate ratio R^θ — in which common normalization largely cancels — they show that combining positions breaks vector-axial and normalization degeneracies that a single spectrum cannot, because the vector-axial interference term B carries a distinct energy dependence. Combining three positions with a 5% correlated normalization benchmark, they project 95% CL NSI sensitivities of −0.07 ≲ ε^{uA}{μμ} ≲ 0.06, −0.10 ≲ ε^{uV}{μμ} ≲ 0.12, and −0.05 ≲ ε^{udL}_{μμ} ≲ 0.05. Axial NC NSI, which do not contribute to the coherent matter potential, are the key complementary target to oscillation and high-energy scattering probes; the left-handed CC coefficient, which merely rescales CCQE, benefits little from multiple positions.

Why it matters: it gives movable/multi-position detectors a genuinely new physics use — spectroscopy of interaction structure — extensible to single-pion channels and other beams. Caveats are substantial and acknowledged: the analysis retains only the dominant ν_μ component, treats oxygen as an incoherent free-nucleon sum with a hard Pauli-blocking cutoff (yielding NCQE rates above NEUT), adopts an 80% flat NCQE efficiency and a schematic 5–10% systematic, and for light mediators the low-Q² cutoff removes most position-dependent discriminating power.

arXiv:2608.05150 §

The QCD Axion and Neutrino Masses

Hridoy Debnath et al.

Debnath, Fileviez Pérez, and Miller perform a unified analysis identifying the PQ-breaking scale with the neutrino-mass-generation scale (M_seesaw ~ v_S ~ f_a) across both DFSZ and KSVZ, for six mechanisms: Type-I/II/III seesaw, the Zee model, and colored seesaw I/II. For each they compute the E/N anomaly ratio and hence g_aγγ. Within DFSZ, Type-I, Type-II and Zee leave E/N unchanged from the minimal model (new fields are singlets or scalars), whereas Type-III’s fermionic electroweak triplets shift E/N (8/3→11/3 for DFSZ-I, 2/3→5/3 for DFSZ-II) — a measurable g_aγγ signature. In KSVZ, colored seesaw I and II predict E/N = 0 and 16/9, giving g_aγγ^I/g_aγγ^II ~ 14. A robust generic prediction across all frameworks is a derivative axion-neutrino coupling ∝ m_ν/f_a. If the axion is dark matter, a→νν decays produce a monochromatic flux peaked at E_ν = m_a/2 lying above both the CNB and solar thermal fluxes, with branching ratios and lifetimes sensitive to the neutrino mass ordering.

Why it matters: it ties the strong-CP solution, neutrino mass, and dark matter to one scale and yields correlated, testable handles — future haloscopes (ADMX, JWST bounds already excise parameter space), a distinguishing g_aγγ per mechanism, and a PTOLEMY-adjacent neutrino signal. Caveats: the axion-neutrino couplings are enormously f_a-suppressed, the neutrino-flux detectability is “very challenging,” and the seesaw–PQ identification is an assumed upper bound on f_a rather than a forced relation.

arXiv:2608.02718 §

The Forward Neutrino Flux and its Secondaries at a 10 TeV Muon Collider

Ju-Yeol Choi et al.

Using a purpose-built Monte Carlo (MINT) that propagates and decays muons through a realistic IMCC hybrid lattice (v0.9 straight section + v0.6 arcs), Choi et al. characterize the forward \(\nu\) flux from the \(\pm180\) m straight sections around the IP. The headline: \(\sim7\times10^{18}\) \(\nu+\bar\nu\)/year through a benchmark 3.2-tonne, 43 g/cm² fiducial volume at 5 km, giving \(\mathcal{O}(10^9)\) neutrino-nucleus interactions/year plus \(\sim5\times10^5\) \(\nu\)-\(e\) scattering events. Crucially, the beam divergence (\(\mathcal{O}(0.1\)–\(1)\) mrad) dominates over the intrinsic \(1/\gamma\sim0.02\) mrad opening angle, washing out the “prism” energy-angle correlation claimed in preliminary studies — a concrete correction to earlier flux modeling. Secondary muons from CC interactions in upstream rock give \(\sim2\) penetrating, >99.9%-polarized muons (\(\langle E_\mu\rangle\simeq1.2\) TeV) per bunch crossing per detector — a background overlapping the neutrino signal in time, but also a potential wide-band polarized TeV muon source. Secondary \(\nu_\tau\) (charm, ITD, tridents, \(D_s^*\)) yield only \(\sim0.2\) CC events/year: unobservable.

Why it matters: this is the concrete simulation behind today’s neutrino-factory framing, quantifying both the physics opportunity (electroweak precision, HNL searches reaching \(|U_{\mu N}|^2\simeq2\times10^{-11}\) near 15 GeV, dipole couplings to \(\sim4\times10^{-10}\) GeV⁻¹) and the radiation/background reality. Caveats are stated plainly: the lattice is optimized for luminosity, not neutrinos; the BSM reach curves are event-yield targets before backgrounds and efficiencies; and recovering the prism effect would need a ~100× larger \(\beta\)-function. Results are declared compatible and complementary to the independent Burk et al. study.

arXiv:2607.28346 §

Collider Spin Tomography with Missing Neutrinos

Tengyu Ai et al.

Ai et al. recast spin tomography under invisible particles as a coarse-grained POVM on the production density matrix, proving that information loss is governed by the kernel of the visible-data map, not by the number of kinematic solutions. Applied analytically to \(e^+e^-\to\tau^+\tau^-\to\pi^+\pi^-\nu\bar\nu\), the twofold ambiguity leaves exactly one unidentifiable direction: the antisymmetric transverse correlation \(C_{nr}-C_{rn}\). The differential rate, both polarization vectors, and the remaining fourteen spin coefficients are all recoverable from the pion distribution alone. They build a template-free, self-consistent fixed-point unfolding where fold weights are set by the data-reconstructed density matrix rather than a flat 50/50 average. Closure tests on \(10^8\) events (\(\sim9.3\) ab⁻¹) show the flat average is visibly biased even in the production-angle distribution, while iterations converge to truth on the identifiable subspace — confirmed in both SM and a deliberately large anomalous tau-dipole benchmark (\(a_\tau=0.01-0.02i\)), proving it is not an SM template fit. Power-iteration/Arnoldi analysis pins the sole marginal (\(\lambda\simeq1\)) mode to \(C_{nr}^-\), with the next eigenvalue \(\simeq0.96\) (contracting).

Why it matters: this extends the collider-quantum-information program (following the \(t\bar t\) tomography flagged two days ago) to final states with missing energy — tau pairs, but also, they note, fully leptonic \(WW\) and dileptonic \(t\bar t\). Entanglement/CHSH claims survive: concurrence and CHSH are reported as positivity-constrained intervals over the null direction, still yielding truth-consistent sensitivity. Caveat: the clean one-dimensional kernel is specific to the \(\pi\nu\) channel; multi-hadron modes require numerical POVMs, and results are closure tests, not full sensitivity projections with detector effects.

arXiv:2608.03261 §

Measurement of forward jet suppression in Pb+Pb collisions at √(s_NN)=5.02 TeV with ATLAS

ATLAS Collaboration

(Abstract only.) ATLAS reports the first inclusive jet \(R_{AA}\) at forward rapidity in heavy-ion collisions, for anti-\(k_t\) \(R=0.4\) jets with \(p_T>74\) GeV in \(2.8<|y|<3.2\) and \(3.2<|y|<3.6\), using 1.72 nb⁻¹ Pb+Pb and 255 pb⁻¹ \(pp\) at 5.02 TeV. Jets are significantly suppressed; suppression increases monotonically from peripheral to central collisions with only mild \(p_T\) dependence. The key comparison: suppression is stronger in \(2.8<|y|<3.2\) than in the previously measured central region \(|y|<2.8\). In the most forward bin (\(3.2<|y|<3.6\)) the precision is insufficient for a conclusive statement.

Why it matters: forward jets probe more asymmetric parton kinematics — different flavor mix (more quark-initiated, higher-\(x\)), different path geometry, and different local medium density — so the stronger-than-central suppression is a genuinely new lever on parton energy loss, complementing today’s small-system QGP thread (ALICE OO strangeness, weekend \(\Upsilon\)/charm-\(v_2\) results). Caveat visible in the abstract: the most-forward bin’s limited statistics leaves the extrapolation of the trend to \(|y|\sim3.4\) open, so the flavor/geometry interpretation rests mainly on the \(2.8<|y|<3.2\) interval.

arXiv:2608.00923 §

Measurement of the Hubble constant with high-energy neutrinos

Gonzalo Herrera et al.

The first-ever \(H_0\) determination from astrophysical neutrinos: Herrera, Kamp and Argüelles build a distance ladder from 12 X-ray–selected Seyferts with IceCube per-source excesses, exploiting the disk-corona relation \(L_\nu=\kappa L_X^\beta\). NGC 1068 (Cepheid+TRGB, \(10.93\pm0.74\) Mpc) anchors the absolute scale; the 11 Hubble-flow sources supply \(H_0\) through the \(d_L^{2(\beta-1)}\) lever arm that only exists when \(\beta\neq1\). Re-extracting signal counts from the 14-year public point-source data with SkyLLH and profiling \(\kappa\) analytically, they find \(H_0=49^{+40}_{-30}\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}\) and \(\beta=0.67^{+0.16}_{-0.25}\), with the calorimetric \(\beta=1\) disfavored at \(\sim2\sigma\) — the first population-level slope measurement, pointing to subcalorimetric coronae (Bethe-Heitler or synchrotron suppression).

Why it matters: this is a genuinely new distance rung immune to dust, absorption, scattering and radiative-transfer systematics — the entire error budget lives in source physics. As the digest frames it, it proposes a new rung rather than kicking away an old one. Caveats are candid and severe: the \(H_0\) uncertainty is enormous and consistent with both Planck and SH0ES at \(1\sigma\); the value swings dramatically with the NGC 1068 intrinsic luminosity choice (\(H_0=109^{+29}_{-37}\) if the high value is adopted) and with adding NGC 4151 as a second anchor (to \(H_0\approx109\), a \(\sim15\%\) residual tension the authors attribute to intrinsic \(L_\nu\)–\(L_X\) scatter). Selection on neutrino significance can also imprint a spurious correlation. IceCube-Gen2 could reach \(\sigma(H_0)\approx16\)–20.

arXiv:2608.01289 §

Combination of vector boson scattering measurements in leptonic final states at √(s)=13 TeV

CMS Collaboration

CMS delivers its most comprehensive VBS combination: seven analyses over the full 138 fb\(^{-1}\) Run 2 dataset, spanning same-sign and opposite-sign WW, WZ, ZZ, plus semileptonic WV/ZV channels, fit simultaneously across 48 phase-space regions and 1525 bins. A 4-POI model returns signal strengths all consistent with unity — \(\mu_{\mathrm{SSWW}}=1.05^{+0.15}_{-0.14}\), \(\mu_{\mathrm{OSWW}}=1.11\pm0.20\) (\(6.4\sigma\)), \(\mu_{\mathrm{WZ}}=1.21^{+0.28}_{-0.25}\) (\(7.0\sigma\)), \(\mu_{\mathrm{ZZ}}=1.15^{+0.44}_{-0.37}\) (\(4.0\sigma\)) — with the combination sharpening OSWW and WZ uncertainties by up to 15% over the best individual inputs. A charge-separated 6-POI model probes the expected production charge asymmetry from the proton’s valence content, finding \(\mu_{W^+W^+}=1.13^{+0.17}_{-0.16}\) versus \(\mu_{W^-W^-}=0.84^{+0.28}_{-0.24}\).

Why it matters: this is the consolidated stress-test of the electroweak-scattering program, coherently accounting for cross-channel correlations that single measurements cannot — the reference point against which unitarity-violating BSM effects in longitudinal boson scattering would appear as event excesses. Everything is SM-consistent, so it functions as a benchmark and a clean baseline for the EFT/anomalous-coupling recasts that follow. No tension is flagged; the correlations among signal strengths are mild.

arXiv:2608.00182 §

Modification of Υ production in pO and OO collisions at LHCb

LHCb collaboration et al.

Abstract only. LHCb measures \(\Upsilon(2S)\) and \(\Upsilon(3S)\) yields relative to \(\Upsilon(1S)\) in \(pp\), \(p\)O, and OO collisions. The \(pp\) ratios match earlier LHCb results; \(p\)O shows only slight relative suppression of the excited states, but OO suppresses the \(\Upsilon(2S)\) by a factor of \(\sim2\), with evidence for \(\Upsilon(3S)\) suppression. The jump from a small \(p\)O effect to significant OO suppression is read as the onset of additional mechanisms — color screening in a deconfined medium — with QGP-formation models successfully describing the OO data.

Why it matters: alongside ALICE’s charm-sector \(v_2\) splitting, this pushes the “how small can QGP be” question onto real Run 3 oxygen data, with sequential melting appearing in a system this small. The caveat, visible even from the abstract, is disentangling cold-nuclear-matter effects from genuine deconfinement screening — the \(p\)O reference is precisely what makes the OO excess interpretable, but the model dependence of the QGP interpretation remains.

arXiv:2607.29138 §

Observation of a Charged Charmoniumlike Structure in e⁺e⁻→D⁰D⁻π⁺⁺c.c.

BESIII Collaboration

BESIII performs a partial-wave analysis of \(e^+e^-\to D^0D^-\pi^+\) on 1667 pb\(^{-1}\) collected at \(\sqrt{s}=4682\) MeV, using combined single- and double-tag reconstruction. A near-threshold enhancement in the \(D^0D^-\) system, modeled with a Flatté lineshape, requires \(J^P=1^-\) and yields mass \((3794.7\pm6.0\pm8.7)\) MeV/\(c^2\) and \(D^0D^-\) coupling \((1.6\pm0.2\pm0.2)\) GeV. The raw fit significance is \(7.7\sigma\); after 320 pseudo-experiments account for the look-elsewhere effect and systematics are folded in, it settles at \(5.3\sigma\) — enough to claim observation of this new charged charmoniumlike state, \(X(3790)^-\). Cross-checks (alternative \(J^P<4\), added PHSP or non-resonant amplitudes) leave the signal at \(\geq8\sigma\) when nuisance components are included. The analysis simultaneously updates the excited charmed mesons, with new \(D_2^*(2460)\), \(D_1^*(2600)\) and \(D_1^*(2760)\) parameters.

Why it matters: this is the first isovector state seen coupling to a \((D\bar D)^\pm\) pair — a channel previously argued unlikely, since spin-parity conservation forbids the \(D\bar D\pi\) vertex under pure pion-exchange binding, yet phenomenology and lattice predict a scalar \(D\bar D\) bound state. A charged \(1^-\) structure right at threshold extends the \(Z_c\)/\(X(3872)\) molecular narrative into a new final state and constrains heavy-quark-symmetry partner counting. Caveats: the fit fraction is small (\(1.3\pm0.3\%\)), systematics on mass are large (\(\pm8.7\) MeV), and the Flatté parameterization plus fixed \(D_3^*(2750)\) and \(\bar D^{*0}\)-width inputs carry model dependence; the state’s nature (molecule vs. threshold cusp) is not resolved here.

arXiv:2607.28722 §

Gravity Probe-DM: The Gravitational Laboratory for Dark Matter

Yu-Dai Tsai et al.

Tsai and Foote propose a purely gravitational Solar-System dark-matter search exploiting the irreducible downstream wake the Sun’s gravity carves into any incident unbound DM flow. Two precision-ranged spacecraft act as a heliocentric gradiometer: a uniform acceleration cancels, but the wake tidal tensor projected along the baseline does not. For a LISA-like \(2.5\times10^6\) km baseline crossing a 0.1 AU coherent width at 30 km/s, a one-percent density excess gives a raw uniform-core range response of only 0.63 pm over the 5.77 d crossing. Because the free response grows as \(T^2\) while the projected tide stays coherent, an axial “wake-skimming” arc lifts this to 2.5 nm over one year; an optimistic low-lag dark-disk benchmark (20% of local density, 30% focused contrast) reaches ~15 nm. REBOUND/REBOUNDx integrations validate the linear-in-density scaling and the \(\dot\omega\propto M_{\rm od}\) precession law.

Why it matters: existing planetary/asteroid-ephemeris bounds only limit smooth, near-spherical DM around the Sun; this concept instead targets an anisotropic, directional structure tied to the Galactic flow, giving a distinctive turn-on/turn-off tidal waveform and, for wave DM, de Broglie-scale interference. A detection would be the first Solar-System gravitational DM map and would calibrate the local phase-space model underlying direct detection. Caveats are substantial and stated plainly: nanometre signals sit beneath solar/planetary/asteroid tides, radiation pressure and thermal recoil; the \(T^2\) enhancement requires a passive one-year dwell that is unavailable near 1 AU without going to several AU; and every number is a pre-fit response scale, not a demonstrated sensitivity. LISA Pathfinder archival data is proposed only as a methodological null test.

arXiv:2607.28761 §

Quantum Tomography of Top Quarks as a Probe of Charge-Parity Violation

Eren Erdogan et al.

Erdogan et al. compute the first fully renormalized one-loop Higgs-induced correction to the \(t\bar t\) production spin density matrix from a CP-mixed top-Yukawa coupling \(\mathcal{L}=-(m_t/v)h\,\bar t(a_t+ib_t\gamma_5)t\), predicting its imprint on all fifteen Fano coefficients. The couplings enter at \(\mathcal{O}(y_t^2/16\pi^2)\); CP-odd effects are linear in \(a_tb_t\) and populate specifically the antisymmetric correlation \(C_{nr}^-\) (dispersive) and the polarization difference \(P_k-\bar P_k\) (absorptive), none of which shift the cross section. On-shell renormalization forces imaginary top field-renormalization constants \(\propto a_tb_t\) — the first complete renormalized CP-odd density matrix. Confronting CMS’s full 15-parameter tomography with its covariance, they find at \(a_t=1\) the bound \(-1.40<b_t<1.23\) (95% CL, inclusive Fano fit), tightening to \(-1.01<b_t<1.26\) when spin and \(m_{t\bar t}\)-shape information are combined — comparable to ATLAS’s \(|b_t|\approx1.1\) from tree-level \(t\bar tH/tH\).

Why it matters: this converts the collider quantum-information program from demonstrating entanglement/magic to a genuine precision measurement, using the reconstructed quantum state itself as the observable. The method is model-agnostic — any operator shifting the density matrix can be confronted with the same tomography without regenerating events. Caveats: the SM sits within 68% CL, so this is a constraint not a hint; the bound assumes all other new physics decouples from the loop; and the existing binning was optimized for quantum-property studies, so finer \(m_{t\bar t}\) bins near threshold — where Higgs corrections peak — would sharpen sensitivity. Two mirror minima under \((a_t,b_t)\to(-a_t,-b_t)\) remain unresolved.

arXiv:2607.28396 §

A measurement of the Higgs boson mass in the diphoton decay channel in proton-proton collisions at √(s)=13 TeV

CMS Collaboration

CMS pins down \(m_H=125.13\pm0.15\) GeV (\(\pm0.10\,\text{stat}\pm0.12\,\text{syst}\)) using the full 138 fb\(^{-1}\) Run 2 diphoton sample — a near factor-of-two systematic reduction over their 2020 result. The key advance is the photon energy scale: rather than extrapolating electron scales from \(Z\to ee\) via simulation (previously the dominant uncertainty), they now measure residual electron-photon differences directly from \(Z\to\mu\mu\gamma\) FSR photons, and apply fine-grained \(E_\text{T}\)-dependent resolution corrections that bridge the gap between \(Z\)-electron energies (\(\approx45\) GeV) and \(H\to\gamma\gamma\) photons (\(\approx60\) GeV). The leading systematics are now the \(Z\to\mu\mu\gamma\) sample statistics (74 MeV), muon momentum scale (55 MeV), and high-\(E_\text{T}\) non-linearity (54 MeV). The signal strength \(\mu=0.95\pm0.06\pm0.07\) is SM-consistent.

Why it matters: this is the most precise single-channel \(m_H\) to date, a direct input to EW global fits and vacuum-stability arguments. Combined with CMS 7+8 TeV diphoton data it gives \(125.06\pm0.14\) GeV. One visible caveat: the 13 TeV value sits 1.2\(\sigma\) above the earlier CMS 7+8 TeV combination, and the result remains systematics-limited, with further gains tied to the statistically starved \(Z\to\mu\mu\gamma\) calibration sample.

arXiv:2607.27315 §

The Very Nearly Right Theory of Flavor

Nima Arkani-Hamed et al.

(Abstract only.) Arkani-Hamed et al. parametrize the ten-dimensional space of quark flavor data using “nine-link textures” — full-rank \(Y_{u,d}\) matrices with nine total nonzero entries and a single CP phase. Fitting all such textures, they find the CP phases cluster tightly around multiples of \(\pi/8\), driven by a “Yukawa triangle” defined by the matrix entries that coincides with the unitarity triangle at leading order in small flavor parameters. The empirical observation being explained is that \((\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\approx(\pi/2,\pi/8,3\pi/8)\). Crucially, the two triangles differ beyond leading order, yielding calculable deviations from these values that the next generation of unitarity-triangle-angle measurements can decisively confirm or exclude.

Why it matters: this turns a striking numerical coincidence into a falsifiable framework, and lands one day after Lin’s geometric CKM reparameterization — two independent attacks on the same pattern. A bonus: the textures are sparse with naturally real determinants, which combined with spontaneous CP violation offers a route to solving strong CP. Caveat: as abstract-only, the robustness of the “clustering” claim and the size of the predicted deviations relative to projected experimental precision cannot be assessed here.

arXiv:2607.27313 §

Suppressed Quantum Effects of Weakly Coupled Waves

Yunjia Bao et al.

Bao et al. prove two general obstructions to detecting nonclassicality in weakly coupled waves (axion DM, gravitational radiation), using the cavity haloscope as worked example. First, detectors couple not to fundamental plane-wave modes but to a coarse-grained “effective mode”; if DM occupies many independent modes, the quantum central limit theorem drives the effective-mode \(P\)-function to a nonnegative thermal Gaussian, erasing negativity regardless of occupancy. They show convergence is rapid — the purity of \(\mathcal N\) coarse-grained Fock states approaches the Gaussian value \(1/(1+2n)\) as \(\sim n(n+1)/[\mathcal N(2n+1)^2]\), fast even for \(n\sim1\). Second, even granting a maximally nonclassical effective mode, all nonclassicality signatures carry extra powers of the tiny conversion efficiency \(\eta=\sin^2(gt_m)\sim10^{-21}\): Mandel \(Q_c=\eta Q_a\geq-\eta\), squeezing \(S_c=\eta S_a\). Distinguishing a Fock from a coherent axion via number statistics needs \(N_\text{shot}\sim1/\eta^2\), i.e. \(t_\text{int}\gtrsim10^{17}\) yr — utterly impractical and unenhanced by large \(N_\text{eff}\).

Why it matters: this is a general no-go closing off the recent high-profile “quantize gravity from GW observations” program, and the cold-water counterpart to yesterday’s Blas–Oller GW\(\to\gamma\gamma\) claim. Distinguishing classical states (coherent vs. Gaussian) remains feasible, needing only \(N_\text{shot}\sim1/\langle n_c\rangle^2\). Caveat honestly flagged: the CLT can be evaded — few contributing modes (fuzzy DM, condensates), surviving inter-mode correlations, or highly monochromatic late-time sources with \(T\Delta\omega\lesssim1\) — though even then the \(\eta\)-suppression persists unless quantum resources scale as \(1/\eta\).

arXiv:2607.27202 §

Anomalies in Hadronic B→VV Decays

Bhubanjyoti Bhattacharya et al.

Bhattacharya et al. extend last year’s charmless \(B\to PP\) SU(3)\(_F\) puzzle to the vector-vector modes, fitting HFLAV/PDG data with reduced matrix elements (equivalently topological diagrams) in the exact SU(3)\(_F\) limit, the only theoretical simplification being neglect of the small Wilson coefficients \(c_7,c_8\) (a ~10% effect). Three fits are staged. The isospin-only \(B\to\rho K^*\) fit is acceptable (\(\chi^2/\text{d.o.f.}=3.6/3\), \(p=0.31\)) — but forces \(|\widetilde{P_{uc}}/\widetilde{P_{tc}}|\sim 200\), some 300× above the expected \(\lesssim0.5\); imposing the physical constraint \(\leq1\) collapses it to \(4.4\sigma\). The combined SU(3)\(_F\) fit over \(V\in\{\rho,K^*\}\) gives \(39.2/5\), a \(5.2\sigma\) discrepancy; adding \(\phi,\omega\) pushes it to \(130/20\), \(p<10^{-15}\), i.e. \(>7\sigma\). The jump is driven partly by \(\mathcal{B}(B^0\to\phi K^{*0})\) and \(\mathcal{B}(B^0\to\omega K^{*0})\) (each contributing >20 to \(\chi^2\)) and by the failure of \(f_L\gg f_T\) in the new modes sharpening tension with \(\Delta S=1\) polarization data.

If it holds, this is a hadronic-amplitude anomaly distinct from the \(b\to s\ell\ell/\nu\bar\nu\) FCNC anomalies — a breakdown of the SM’s flavor-symmetry structure in tree/penguin dominated decays. The result’s strength is its minimal theory input; its acknowledged vulnerability is that only exact SU(3)\(_F\) was tested. The authors concede that whether realistic ~30% SU(3)\(_F\)-breaking can absorb a \(7\sigma\) gap “must be checked,” though they judge it unlikely. Robustness was verified with three independent codes and tens of thousands of seeds.

arXiv:2607.26195 §

First Search for Quirks at the LHC with FASER

FASER Collaboration

FASER delivers the first LHC quirk search, using 186 fb\(^{-1}\) of 13.6 TeV \(pp\) data (2022–2024). Quirks — pairs charged under a QCD-like infracolor SU(\(N_\text{IC}=2\)) with right-handed-lepton SM charges — are Drell-Yan produced forward, remain bound by an unbreakable IC string, and arrive at FASER (480 m downstream) slow and out-of-time. The signature is a pair depositing ~2 MIPs of charge in all eight scintillators with reconstructed speed \(0.6<\beta<1\) and arrival time \(t_0\) offset from the nominal bunch. Backgrounds (through-going muons via an ABCD estimate, muon-induced “kinked” showers) total well below one event; the signal region contains zero observed events. Using a CL\(_s\) fit, masses up to 179 GeV are excluded at \(\Lambda=10\) keV, and for the first time weak-scale quirk masses are excluded across the broad range \(300\,\text{eV}<\Lambda<100\,\text{keV}\).

This opens a genuinely new frontier: non-Abelian hidden sectors (motivated by neutral naturalness and IC-glueball dark matter) had no prior dedicated LHC search, only the DØ Tevatron limit. FASER’s forward, low-background geometry turns the quirks’ slowness into a clean discriminant. Caveats are honest and intrinsic: only ~0.03–0.04% of produced quirks reach the fiducial region, with reconstruction/selection efficiency ~1% (10%) at \(m_\mathcal{Q}=50\) (175) GeV, and the signal normalization carries a ~25% ISR/FSR modeling uncertainty (up to 28% simulation-statistics uncertainty at small \(\Lambda\)). This is half of Run 3, so reach will grow.

Improved HH→bb̅τ⁺τ⁻ Analysis with 196 fb⁻¹

ATLAS Collaboration

(Abstract only.) ATLAS updates its non-resonant di-Higgs search in \(b\bar b\tau\tau\), combining 140 fb\(^{-1}\) at 13 TeV with 56 fb\(^{-1}\) at 13.6 TeV plus reconstruction and analysis improvements. The signal strength is \(\mu_{HH}=2.6^{+1.4}_{-1.0}\), an observed (expected) significance of 2.6 (1.2)\(\sigma\) over background-only, and 1.65\(\sigma\) above the SM prediction — a mild upward pull consistent with the SM. The self-coupling modifier is constrained to \(\kappa_\lambda\in[-3.4,1.6]\cup[5.5,10.1]\) observed (\([-1.7,8.5]\) expected) at 95% CL. The excess is modest — the SM lies comfortably within 1.65\(\sigma\) — so no anomaly is claimed. The validation channels demonstrate the method’s control, though the observed ZH pull sits above expectation and ZZ below, indicating the measurements remain statistics-limited.

High-energy neutrino emission from the Milky Way

R. Abbasi et al.

Abstract only. Combining all three neutrino-flavor event morphologies with updated ice modelling, calibration and reconstruction over 12 years of IceCube data, a predefined global analysis establishes high-energy neutrino emission from the Galactic plane at 5.7σ. ATLAS Collaboration

Abstract only. ATLAS observes the electroweak production of two isolated photons plus two jets at 6.2σ, with a fiducial cross section of \(13.8^{+3.0}_{-2.6}\) fb, sitting below but consistent with the SM prediction of \(17.1 \pm 2.4\) fb. arXiv:2607.24930 §

Gravitational waves decay in vacuum: a low-energy effect of quantized gravitation

Diego Blas et al.

Blas and Oller argue that GWs, treated as coherent graviton states rather than classical fields, decay into photon pairs via graviton fusion (\(gg\to\gamma\gamma\), Skobelev amplitude) — a process forbidden classically and semiclassically, vanishing as \(\hbar\to0\), and enhanced by the graviton number squared \(N^2\). The key technical point (Supplement SA): the coherent-state mode cannot propagate in the internal graviton line because momentum conservation forces it on-shell at \(s=0\), where the derivative coupling vanishes. The radiated power scales as \(W_\gamma \propto G^2 E_{\rm rel}^2\omega_s^2\sigma_\omega^2\), giving a compact form \(W_\gamma=\tfrac{3}{50\pi}(r_m/\ell)^2\hbar\omega^2\). They derive first constraints on recurrent GW populations from CMB \(\mu\)/\(y\) distortions (COBE/FIRAS: \(|\mu|<9\times10^{-5}\), \(|y|<1.5\times10^{-5}\)), UV/X-ray and \(\gamma\)-ray backgrounds, and D/⁴He photofission, all cast as bounds on \(\sqrt{\Gamma}\,\epsilon E_{\rm rel}/M_\odot\) vs \(\omega_s\).

Why it matters: a genuinely new, purely quantum-gravitational handle on GW sources, whose detection would evidence quantized gravity. The honest caveat is front and center: for astrophysical binaries the photon luminosity is minuscule (comparable to Hawking radiation of the equivalent mass, number luminosity \(\sim10^6\,{\rm s}^{-1}\) at merger), and SGWB depletion is negligible (\(\sim10^{-110}\,{\rm m}^{-3}{\rm s}^{-1}\); \(|\Phi|\approx10^{-25}\,{\rm Hz}^{-1}\) to the GUT scale). Only in ultralight-dark-matter scenarios, where huge occupation numbers boost \(gg\leftrightarrow\phi\phi\), do rates reach \(\sim10^{54}\,{\rm s}^{-1}\) — and that requires the exotic light-scalar sector to exist.

(Abstract only.) arXiv:2607.24494 §

Implications of K→πνν̅ for new physics in B decays

Lukas Allwicher et al.

Allwicher and Bordone propagate the new NA62 \(\mathcal{B}(K^+\to\pi^+\nu\bar\nu)=9.6^{+1.9}_{-1.8}\times10^{-11}\) (sub-20% precision) into two BSM frameworks. Why it matters: this is exactly the cross-mode phenomenology the digest has tracked since the NA62 result — one kaon branching ratio now feeds correlated predictions for \(B\to K^{(*)}\nu\bar\nu\), \(B_s\to\mu\mu\), and future \(K_L\to\pi^0\nu\bar\nu\) (KOTO II). arXiv:2607.22358 §

First Lattice QCD Determination of Lepton-Flavor-Universality Ratios in Light-Meson Leptonic Decays

Peter Boyle et al.

(abstract only) Boyle et al. deliver the first lattice QCD+QED calculation of the structure-dependent (SD) radiative correction to \(R_{e/\mu}\) — the electron-to-muon leptonic-width ratio — for both \(\pi\) and \(K\), working at the physical pion mass and in the continuum limit. Using the infinite-volume reconstruction method with Coulomb-gauge photons to suppress statistical noise and finite-volume effects, they obtain SM predictions \(R_{e/\mu}^\pi = 1.23501(10)\times10^{-4}\) and \(R_{e/\mu}^K = 2.47653(34)\times10^{-5}\) — sub-permille precision on the previously dominant hadronic uncertainty.

Why it matters: \(R_{e/\mu}\) is a benchmark LFU test, and the \(O(0.1\%)\) SD correction was the limiting theory input as PIONEER and NA62 push toward matching precision. This calculation supplies exactly that ingredient from first principles, replacing model-dependent estimates. It is the SM number those experiments will now quote against. As an abstract-only entry, the systematic budget of the continuum/QED extrapolations isn’t visible here, but the quoted errors already sit well below current experimental sensitivity.

arXiv:2607.22193 §

Boosted H→ττ cross-section measurements with ATLAS

ATLAS Collaboration

(abstract only) ATLAS reports evidence for Higgs production at \(p_T^H > 300\) GeV in the di-hadronic-\(\tau\) final state, combining full Run 2 (140 fb\(^{-1}\), 13 TeV) with a partial Run 3 sample (162 fb\(^{-1}\), 13.6 TeV). Standard hadronic-\(\tau\) reconstruction loses efficiency when the two \(\tau\)s collapse into a single large-radius jet, so the analysis deploys dedicated boosted di-\(\tau\) reconstruction and identification plus multivariate discriminants. The inclusive boosted measurement reaches observed (expected) significance of 3.8 (3.3)σ — evidence-level.

Why it matters: this extends \(H\to\tau\tau\) sensitivity into a high-\(p_T\) regime that probes the Higgs coupling structure and is sensitive to BSM contributions that grow with momentum transfer. Results are cast in the simplified-template-cross-section framework, with differential \(p_T^H\) measurements for gluon fusion and an inclusive VBF cross section — directly usable for EFT interpretation. The observed significance modestly exceeds expectation, consistent with SM within the still-limited boosted statistics.

arXiv:2607.21629 §

Scattering Amplitudes as Programs: Self-Evolving Search for Theory and Event Generation

Yi Gu et al.

Gu and Krippendorf treat amplitude evaluation as a program-search problem: a repository-scale coding agent (OpenAI Codex) acts as mutation operator inside an external generate–evaluate–select loop with frozen correctness gates and objectives, evolving executable amplitude programs. Three benchmarks. (1) Scaling: from a BCFW seed, search rediscovers Berends–Giele currents, then CSW, then a fixed-\(k\) split-helicity transfer dynamic program with \(O(n)\) source work, hitting an 805× geometric-mean speed-up over 34 timing cells. (2) Structural: NMHV rational forms are reorganised into R-invariant-style cells and glued supercells, lifting a cancellation-conditioning score from 0.376 to 0.605 and collapsing 8–13 terms to 2–3. (3) Tree complexity: across twenty QCD/EW processes, exact arithmetic falls 47.7× (geometric mean 11.68×), with a 5.9× post-hoc Python runtime gain. In matched pure-gluon tests (\(n=4\to6\)), the evolved engine’s lead over a Sherpa-Comix exact-sum call grows 10×→277×, while its gap to -O3-compiled MadGraph Fortran narrows 77.7×→13.7×. Total cost: 115 candidates, ~$112, 8.15 agent-hours.

Why it matters: with HL-LHC projections assigning ~20% of ATLAS CPU to event generation, matrix-element kernels are a real bottleneck, and this shows agent-driven search can traverse genuine changes of representation — not just tuning — with fully archived, inspectable lineages. This is the digest’s “agents orchestrating” thread made concrete. Caveats are stated plainly: these are tree-level, single-stack, isolated-kernel benchmarks; the Comix comparison targets exact colour/helicity sums (Comix can sample), no symbolic divisibility proof accompanies the gluing, and native generator integration plus end-to-end throughput remain untested.

Friday, July 24, 2026

arXiv:2607.21492 §

A converged architecture for processing 32 Tbps of physics data in real-time at the LHCb experiment

Roel Aaij et al.

The upgraded LHCb now reads out its entire detector — roughly 30 MHz of non-empty pp collisions, 32 Tbps — with no hardware low-level trigger, a factor-40 increase in data rate over the previous system. The engineering result: the full stream is filtered in real time on just 164 nodes (2× AMD EPYC 7502, 2× NVIDIA RTX A5000 each), applying a 30:1 reduction in HLT1 entirely on GPU. The design rests on three moves: commercial-off-the-shelf networking (an InfiniBand HDR fat-tree treating event-building as an all-to-all personalized exchange), second-scale host-memory buffering that eliminates incast without deep-buffer switches, and a “converged” node hosting readout, event-building, and GPU filtering together via zero-copy/RDMA. Algorithmically, a multi-event GPU scheduler batches ~600–800 events per sequence, with mixed-precision reconstruction (single precision for a truncated-region Kalman filter; half precision for VELO storage and SciFi seeding). They report 41 MHz peak combined throughput, 36% headroom over the 30 MHz requirement, and demonstrate a full EB+HLT1 integration test sustaining 160 GB/s memory and 115 GB/s PCIe per node with no performance drops.

Why it matters: this is the production endpoint of the month’s real-time-reconstruction wave (the July 17 GPU-Kalman work), and the comparison table makes the claim concrete — the highest software-processed data rate of any experiment, exceeding ATLAS/CMS (1.6 Tbps into software after a 300× L1 cut) and ALICE (7.2 Tbps). Removing the low-level trigger reduces selection bias by considering the whole event. It’s an engineering demonstration paper rather than a physics measurement; the throughput figures are commissioning-phase benchmarks.

arXiv:2607.20635 §

What could an emerging Big Bang Nucleosynthesis discrepancy be hinting at?

Vivian Poulin et al.

Using PRIMAT (rewritten in Python) with the Cooke et al. deuterium measurement (10⁵D/H = 2.527±0.030), the BBN-inferred baryon density ω_b ≃ 0.0219 sits ~2.1σ below the ΛCDM CMB value and ~3.1σ below the higher ω_b = 0.02272±0.00014 preferred in EDE fits — early-dark-energy solutions to H₀ generically raise ω_b and thus worsen the deuterium problem. The authors test three expansion histories against the EDE ω_b target: ΛCDM, a ΔN_eff extension, and a transient “very early dark energy” (vEDE) component peaking during deuterium burning. Constant extra radiation fails (Q_DMAP = 3.1σ, ΔAIC ≃ +1.2): it raises Y_p too efficiently at weak freeze-out. vEDE succeeds, because a boost localized at T_D ≃ 0.03 MeV shortens the residual deuterium-burning window (raising D/H at large ω_b) while barely touching ⁴He synthesis. The combined fit gives ΔH/H(T_D) = 0.087⁺⁰·⁰³⁶₋₀.₀₃₇, no residual tension (Q_DMAP = 0.7σ, ΔAIC ≃ −5.3).

Why it matters: this is the natural referee’s-follow-up to the H₀ World Cup — the very models that win on H₀ lose on primordial deuterium, and the resolution demands yet another light scalar, framed as an axiverse window at the BBN epoch. Caveats are candidly stated: the CMB is compressed to a single Gaussian ω_b likelihood (not a joint fit); nuclear-rate uncertainties enter only through tabulated MC errors; the discrepancy is partly rate-driven (PArthENoPE agrees better with the data), and the updated D/H = 2.508 softens the ΛCDM tension to 2.7σ.

arXiv:2607.21244 §

Hubble tension: the shape wall

Miguel A. Sabogal et al.

The standard no-go against late-time H₀ solutions is a normalization wall (BAO fixes r_d·H₀). This paper erects an independent shape wall: at fixed acoustic scale θ_s and sound horizon, δH₀/H₀ ≃ δI/I, where I ≡ ∫dz/E(z). Using Gaussian-process reconstructions of the dimensionless expansion rate E(z) from unanchored SNeIa (PantheonPlus, DESY5Dvk, Union3) and DESI DR2 BAO — marginalizing over the r_d·h amplitude to retain only shape information — they find E(z) can deviate at most ≈5–7% from fiducial ΛCDM. The directly-constrained low-redshift integral I_< (z ≲ 2.3) is pinned to the percent level: δI_</I_< ≲ 0.5% (68%), ≲1.5% (95%) across SNeIa+BAO combinations, mapping to (δH₀/H₀)_< ≲ 0.8% at 95%. Adding a model-guided estimate of the unconstrained high-z tail I_> (from ΛCDM/wCDM fits) yields a total δH₀/H₀ ≲ 2% (conservative) to ≲3.7% (maximally permissive) — versus the ≈9.3% needed to close the tension, leaving ≳4.3σ residual.

Why it matters: alongside Poulin et al., this is the day’s second wall against tension-solvers, and it is statistically stronger than the normalization wall since it survives redshift-independent BAO recalibration. The key methodological point — pointwise E(z) envelopes overstate freedom because integrating I preserves cross-redshift correlations — is well argued. Honest caveats: I_> bounds are model-dependent (not nonparametric); flat FLRW, smoothness, and the distance-duality relation are assumed. The explicit surviving loophole is new physics at z ≪ 0.01, below SNeIa reach.
