arXiv:2607.20246 §

Evidence for longitudinally polarised Z bosons in electroweak W^±Zjj production

ATLAS Collaboration

Abstract only. ATLAS reports 4.0σ evidence for a longitudinally polarised \(Z\) in electroweak \(W^\pm Zjj\) production, combining 140 fb\(^{-1}\) at 13 TeV and 164 fb\(^{-1}\) at 13.6 TeV in the fully leptonic (\(e/\mu\)) channel. The integrated fiducial fraction is \(f_{X0}=0.32\pm0.09\), consistent with the NLO-EW Standard Model prediction. Upper limits at 95% CL of 0.19 and 0.12 are set on longitudinally polarised \(W\) and on doubly-longitudinal \(WZ\) events, respectively. The 13.6 TeV fiducial cross-section is measured as \(0.374\pm0.077\,(\text{stat})\pm0.053\,(\text{syst})\pm0.009\,(\text{lumi})\) fb per lepton flavour.

Why it matters: This is the direct sequel to last week’s doubly-longitudinal \(ZZ\) observation, extending the electroweak-symmetry-breaking polarization program into the VBS-adjacent \(WZjj\) sector — the channel most sensitive to longitudinal-boson scattering that probes unitarization by the Higgs. That the single-longitudinal \(Z\) fraction sits squarely on the SM prediction, while the doubly-longitudinal component remains only bounded (\(<0.12\)), reflects the statistical reach of current datasets rather than any tension. The caveat is intrinsic: a 4.0σ result on \(f_{X0}\) with \(\pm0.09\) precision and the merged two-energy dataset means the harder doubly-longitudinal target awaits Run 3 luminosity.

arXiv:2607.19948 §

First Measurement of the Relative Phase between Proton Psionic Form Factors

BESIII Collaboration et al.

What they did: Using \(10.09\times10^9\) \(J/\psi\) events, BESIII measured the normal polarization \(\mathcal{P}_y\) of protons from \(e^+e^-\to J/\psi\to p\bar p\) by exploiting secondary \(pp\) elastic scattering off hydrogen in the beam-pipe mineral-oil layer and MDC inner wall — turning the spectrometer itself into a polarimeter. Requiring a third track (the knocked-out target proton), vetoing quasi-free nuclear collisions via \(|p_\text{target}|<50\) MeV/\(c\), they isolate 2556 elastic events (15.2% efficiency) and extract, via the SAID analyzing powers, \(\sin\Delta\Phi=-0.20\pm0.34_\text{stat}\pm0.11_\text{syst}\) — the first determination of the relative phase between the proton’s psionic (complex \(G_E^\psi\), \(G_M^\psi\)) form factors.

Why it matters: The relative TL-region phase had been unreachable for nucleons, which lack self-analyzing decays; hyperons (\(\Lambda\), \(\Sigma\), \(\Xi\)) had been mapped but the proton and neutron remained blank. Placing the proton near \(\Sigma^{+/0}\) and below \(\Lambda\), \(\Xi^{-/0}\) gives a first SU(3)-symmetry test point. The method — validated by a pseudo-experiment recovering an injected \(\sin\Delta\Phi=-0.84\) as \(-0.82\pm0.02\) — is reusable at any large-acceptance spectrometer, with \(\Sigma^-\) and (with better neutron detection) the neutron as obvious next targets. Caveats: the result is statistics-limited and consistent with zero; two \(\Delta\Phi\) solutions remain degenerate, resolvable only with a polarized beam (\(\mathcal{P}_x\)) or antiproton polarization, e.g. at STCF.

arXiv:2607.19487 §

Four, One, and None: Quantifying the UHE Neutrino Anomaly Across ANITA-IV, KM3NeT, and IceCube

Dibya S. Chattopadhyay et al.

What they did: Chattopadhyay, Argüelles, and Brdar perform the first joint statistical fit of the four near-horizon ANITA-IV events, the single KM3-230213A event, and IceCube’s null result, using semi-analytic energy-, direction-, and time-dependent effective areas for all three detectors with realistic exposures (26.6 d for ANITA-IV, 287 d for ARCA-21, 5400 d for IceCube). For a common diffuse power-law flux, no normalization/spectral-index choice reproduces the observed \((4,0,1)\) counts: the best fit predicts ~5 events at IceCube and \(\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})\)/\(\mathcal{O}(10^{-1})\) at ANITA-IV/KM3NeT, a 7.5σ tension (7.9σ for fixed \(E^{-2}\)). Modeling rare all-sky transients, with the probability of four favorable ANITA-IV overlaps fixed to \(\sim10^{-4}\) and scanning \(10^5\) MC realizations, the least-anomalous benchmark (185 sources, 10-day duration) still leaves 5.9σ.

Why it matters: This converts the qualitative ANITA/KM3NeT-vs-IceCube “puzzle” into a hard number, sharpening the KM3-230213A thread. Crucially, the tension is driven by ANITA-IV, not KM3NeT: the ANITA-IV–IceCube-only fit gives 5.8–7.8σ, while KM3-230213A alone is a mild 1.8–2.5σ. The authors conclude neither diffuse flux nor rare identical transients works within the SM, pointing to structured source populations, detector/classification effects, or new physics. Caveats: the effective areas are semi-analytic, reproducing collaboration values only within a factor ~2 near the horizon; the trigger-efficiency parametrization and spherical detector approximation (especially for the anisotropic ARCA-21) carry acknowledged modeling uncertainty.

arXiv:2607.19319 §

Dark matter searches with a 13 meV threshold superconducting sensor array

Christopher Albert et al.

QUALIPHIDE-FIR ran a 41-pixel array of aluminum microwave kinetic inductance detectors (MKIDs) — FIR astronomy photon-counters, peak-tuned at 25 µm/50 meV — as a dual-mode dark matter search with a 13 meV threshold, the lowest yet in a direct-detection experiment. The array couples to a gold-plated spherical dish (5163 mm²) that converts hidden-photon DM into real photons collected at the focus; four on-focus pixels see any signal while 37 off-focus pixels build a data-driven background model, giving genuine discovery potential rather than upper limits only. A blind analysis of 22 hours found no excess (peak local significance 1.33σ at 82 meV), setting the strongest hidden-photon kinetic-mixing limits over 13–90 meV, reaching χ = 1.5×10⁻¹² at 50 meV and improving on prior terrestrial bounds above 90 meV by >20×. The same Al inductors serve as scattering targets: with 1.33 ng·day exposure and the optimal-interval method, the search reaches DM masses of 5 MeV (nuclear) and 20 keV (electronic), roughly an order of magnitude below QROCODILE’s 0.11 eV threshold, carving out new sub-overburden parameter space.

Why it matters: this is the diversifying-detection theme with real limits, opening the meV “THz gap” where cavity and TPC methods fail. The 13 meV threshold also probes the low-energy-excess background at single-phonon energies (below Al’s ~41 meV maximum) for the first time. Caveats are honest: the dominant efficiency systematic η₀ = 0.46±0.06 lumps uncharacterized losses; resolution above 70 meV is uncalibrated (treated as a single counting bin); and the projected scattering sensitivity assumes an aggressive 1000× background reduction, versus the ~10× already demonstrated.

arXiv:2607.19055 §

Prospects for K_L→π⁰νν̅, K_S→μ⁺μ⁻, K_L→π⁰ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ and ε'/ε after the new K⁺→π⁺νν̅ result from NA62

Monika Blanke et al.

Abstract only. Blanke et al. take yesterday’s NA62 result — a \(K^+\to\pi^+\nu\bar\nu\) branching ratio (2016–2024 data) fully consistent with the precise SM prediction — and ask what room remains for its neutral counterpart. In the same scenario \(K_S\to\mu^+\mu^-\) (LHCb) and \(K_L\to\pi^0\ell^+\ell^-\) (KOTO II) are strongly enhanced and the Dual-QCD \(\varepsilon'/\varepsilon\) anomaly is removed, illustrated with a specific \(Z'\) model.

Why it matters: this is the direct phenomenology sequel to the NA62 golden-mode measurement, showing that an SM-like charged channel does not close the door on KOTO II’s discovery target — the golden \(K_L\) mode retains up to tenfold enhancement. The construction is explicitly a proof-of-existence within a chosen \(Z'\) framework, and hinges on tuning new phases plus right-handed currents; the authors note the final NA62 result (2016–2026) could still shift.

arXiv:2607.19254 §

Astrophysical Neutrino Sources as Colliders

Carlos A. Arg\"uelles et al.

Argüelles et al. present a recipe to extract two-sided bounds on the inelastic \(pp\) and \(p\gamma\) cross sections from neutrino point sources by independently pinning every astrophysical input — cosmic-ray luminosity (bounded above by Eddington/relativistic-beaming limits, below by the power needed to sustain observed neutrino and X-ray fluxes) and target column density (from X-ray spectroscopy, Thomson depth, or variability) — leaving \(\sigma\) as the only free parameter. Applied to IceCube’s TXS 0506+056, NGC 1068, the Galactic plane, the stacked 11-source Seyfert population, and KM3NeT’s 220-PeV KM3-230213A, the bounds span \(\sqrt{s}\sim1\) GeV to \(\sim10^5\) GeV. Concrete bands: TXS 10-year data gives \(\sigma_{pp}\simeq0.5\)–70 mb at \(\sqrt{s}\simeq0.9\)–2.8 TeV; the Seyfert stack gives \(\sigma_{pp}\in[2.3,70]\) mb and \(\sigma_{p\gamma}\in[0.08,9]\) mb with the smallest astrophysical uncertainties. Dedicated Pythia 8 simulations revise the per-flavor neutrino energy fraction upward to \(\xi_\nu^{pp}\simeq0.086\)–0.099, ~1.8× the canonical leading-pion estimate, relaxing lower bounds by nearly 2×. Several bounds are more stringent than the Froissart–Martin unitarity limit, and \(p\gamma\) constraints reach beyond HERA’s \(\sqrt{s}\simeq209\) GeV.

Why it matters: it reframes neutrino telescopes as flux-based hadronic colliders scanning continuously in \(\sqrt{s}\), complementary to the shape-based (flux-independent) Auger air-shower method and to collider fixed points. The SM cross section sits inside every band — a nontrivial consistency check. Caveats are prominent: band widths (factors of ~30) are set by astrophysical systematics, not neutrino statistics; TXS relies on an upper-bound-only column density and requires super-Eddington CR power even after beaming correction; and KM3/UHE bounds are projections under a “standard-candle” idealization awaiting source association.

arXiv:2607.16413 §

Measurement of the branching ratio of the K⁺→π⁺νν̅ decay

NA62 Collaboration

NA62 reports \(\mathcal{B}_{2023\text{-}2024}(K^+\to\pi^+\nu\bar\nu)=(7.2^{+2.3}_{-2.1})\times10^{-11}\) from 33 candidates against \(22.9\pm1.1\) SM signal and \(12.0^{+2.9}_{-2.2}\) background events, then combines with 2016–2022 to obtain \((9.6^{+1.9}_{-1.8})\times10^{-11}\) — 20% precision and consistent with SM predictions near \(8\times10^{-11}\). Across 2016–2024, 84 observed events against \(30^{+4}_{-3}\) background give background-only rejection exceeding \(6\sigma\), and the SM-observation significance passes \(5\sigma\) for the first time. The 2023–2024 data were taken at lower intensity (440 MHz vs. the 580 MHz design max), sitting on a broad signal-per-spill maximum around 450 MHz where DAQ dead time and accidental vetoes are balanced; this improved the random-veto efficiency by 1.14 and roughly halved several relative backgrounds. New deep-learning tools — a transformer-based 4D GTK tracker (false-track rate 13%→4%, purity 87%→97%) and a ResNet-based calorimetric \(\pi/\mu\) classifier (~2% higher pion ID, 40% lower \(\mu\to\pi\) misID) — sharpen the selection. A KTAG upgrade to H\(_2\) radiator cut material budget from 3.9% to 0.7% X\(_0\).

Why it matters: this is now the world’s most precise handle on the theoretically cleanest FCNC kaon mode (SM intrinsic uncertainty ~3%, dominated by long-distance charm), and it lands squarely on the SM. The dominant background remains upstream (accidental \(K^+\)/π matching), estimated fully data-driven with ~30% uncertainty. NA62 expects a further ~50% more signal events after 2025–2026 running.

arXiv:2607.17850 §

First Observation of an Exotic Reggeon (G. D. Alexeev (for JPAC Collaboration) et al.)

(Abstract only.) COMPASS presents high-statistics measurements of peripheral \(\eta\pi^-\) and \(\eta'\pi^-\) production in \(\pi^- p\to\eta^{(\prime)}\pi^- p\), performing for the first time an unbinned analysis of the high-mass region to disentangle the exchange mechanisms governing production. arXiv:2607.18233 §

Theoretical uncertainties in reconstructing model parameters with gravitational waves from supercooled phase transitions

Maciej Kierkla et al.

Taking the classically conformal U(1)\(_X\) model as representative of strongly supercooled transitions, the authors scan \(\{g_X,M_X\}\) over \(\mathcal{O}(10^4)\) points and compute GW spectra with two nucleation-rate schemes: a 4D daisy-resummed potential with dimensionally-estimated prefactor, and a full NLO calculation (two-loop 3D-EFT matching plus exact scalar and vector fluctuation determinants, “NLOdet”). Injecting the resulting signals into LISA and running a Fisher-matrix reconstruction of the fundamental parameters, they find the daisy scheme strongly disfavored: the two methods disagree by \(\mathcal{O}(10\%)\) in the reconstructed gauge coupling \(g_X\), an order of magnitude larger than the daisy RG-scale ambiguity (\(\mathcal{O}(1\%)\)) and more than an order of magnitude above the sub-percent Fisher reconstruction uncertainty. The hierarchy is \(\delta_g^{\rm exp}\sim\delta_g^{\bar\mu,\rm NLOdet}\ll\delta_g^{\bar\mu,\rm daisy}\ll\delta_g^{\rm meth}\sim\mathcal{O}(10\%)\). Crucially, no reasonable RG-scale choice reconciles daisy with NLOdet — contradicting a recent claim that scale-shifting suffices — because the discrepancy stems from genuinely missing higher-order physics (the scale-shifting gauge modes require exact determinants, not a derivative expansion). \(M_X\) is essentially unaffected, since it just sets the low-\(T\) Hubble scale.

Why it matters: this extends the digest’s recurring message — GW “signals” from first-order transitions hinge on poorly controlled non-equilibrium/thermal inputs. For high-SNR LISA targets, theoretical diligence, not detector sensitivity, limits the inverse problem. Caveats: dynamical prefactor uses the no-damping approximation (neglecting back-reaction), and the hierarchy inverts in the lower-SNR PTA band.

Track fitting at the full LHC collision rate

Pierre Billoir et al.

(Abstract only.) LHCb deployed a fast parameterised Kalman filter in the first trigger stage from the 2025 run, fitting charged-particle trajectories at the full 30 MHz collision rate on ~500 GPUs. A Precise Determination of the Shape of the Proton's Gluon Cloud from HERA data

Tobias Toll et al.

(Abstract only.) An analytic leading-twist calculation of coherent and incoherent exclusive \(J/\psi\) photoproduction cross sections in the hotspot model is fit to all H1 and ZEUS diffractive data, yielding \(\chi^2/\mathrm{ndf}=0.77\). arXiv:2607.14243 §

Searching for the N-naturalness tower of neutrinos

Sofia Lonardi et al.

First experimental test of the \(N\)-naturalness neutrino tower, via a global fit of public data (Daya Bay, GERDA, LEGEND-200) plus projections (JUNO+TAO, LEGEND-1000) in the Newtrinos.jl framework. The model solves the hierarchy problem with \(N\) dark sectors of varying Higgs mass, encoding residual fine-tuning in a parameter \(r\) (\(r=1\) = no tuning); implemented via the many-mixing-partners mechanism, it generates suppressed neutrino masses and a tower of extra light states that distort oscillations and shift \(m_{\beta\beta}\). Numerically diagonalising the full \(N\)-sector mass matrix (avoiding the perturbative breakdown at small \(N\)), they set 90% CL contours. For Majorana neutrinos, current GERDA+Daya Bay data already exclude non-fine-tuned scenarios (\(r=1\)) up to \(N=10^4\) for both orderings — killing the tidy \(N=10^4\) GUT-unification benchmark that let the framework address the hierarchy problem without ad-hoc tuning. JUNO+TAO projections push lower bounds to \(N\gtrsim500\) and require \(r\lesssim0.1\) in \(500\le N\le10^4\).

Why it matters: it converts a “no-fine-tuning” selling point into a falsified benchmark using existing neutrino data, exactly the constraining mood of today’s DM-and-naturalness cluster. Caveats are honestly flagged: the Dirac case is far weaker (no \(0\nu\beta\beta\) handle, excluded only to \(N\sim50\)); results assume \(m_0=0.01\) eV and \(\eta=1+1/N\); and \(m_{\beta\beta}\) rests on a single benchmark set of nuclear matrix elements (varied only for robustness checks).

arXiv:2607.13283 §

The H_0 World Cup II: A comprehensive competition between proposed Hubble tension solutions

Nils Sch\"oneberg et al.

(Abstract only.) An updated systematic bake-off — the sequel to the “\(H_0\) Olympics” — subjecting fourteen representative alternatives to \(\Lambda\)CDM to a common analysis of current CMB (ACT/SPT), BAO (DESI) and Type Ia SN data, now with the Hubble tension quoted above \(7\sigma\). The contenders span four mechanisms: late-time expansion modifications, modified recombination, extra pre-recombination radiation, and early non-radiative energy injection. Every model is scored with both Bayesian and frequentist tension/performance metrics, with the neutrino mass sum left free, and tested for whether adding curvature or CPL dark energy improves the fit. Leading contenders face a robustness gauntlet: alternative CMB likelihoods and multipole cuts, different SN samples, LSS information, and BBN constraints.

Per the digest, the verdict favors early dark energy and early modified gravity (shifting \(H_0\) toward 70 and cutting the SH0ES residual to \(\sim2.5\)–\(3.6\sigma\)), places varying-electron-mass models intermediate, and finds enhanced-radiation and late-time scenarios failing to beat \(\Lambda\)CDM. Why it matters: it converts a scattered literature of individual tension “solutions” into a refereed, common-framework ranking — the reference tournament model-builders will cite, with Paper II’s robustness tests being the operative part. The honest caveat is intrinsic to any such contest: conclusions depend on the chosen dataset combination and priors, which is precisely why the robustness suite is included; results not visible here beyond the digest summary.

arXiv:2607.13128 §

Dark Energy and Neutrino Flavor from the Weak Axion

Pedro Bittar et al.

Bittar and Wagner identify dark energy with a “weak axion” — the phase of a complex scalar carrying the SM’s anomalous \(U(1)_{B+L}\) charge — and show its potential can be radiatively stable at the meV scale. Electroweak-instanton contributions come out \(\sim10\) orders of magnitude too small (\(\Lambda_{\rm inst}^4\simeq(6\times10^{-6}\,{\rm eV})^4|c_{BL}|^3\)), so the dominant potential arises from two inequivalent Weinberg operators generating an axion-dependent Majorana mass. A spontaneously-broken \(S_3\) lepton permutation symmetry enforces the orthogonality condition \({\rm Tr}[M_\Phi^\dagger M_\star]=0\), which simultaneously kills the quadratic divergence and the leading \(T_\nu^2 m_\nu^2\) thermal force, pushing the first axion-dependent Coleman-Weinberg term to quartic order in neutrino masses. The amplitude vanishes in the exact tribimaximal limit, so the observed departure sets the scale: \(\Lambda_\nu\simeq2.3\,{\rm meV}\sqrt{|\cos\delta_{\rm CP}/0.52+\sin(\theta_{23}-\pi/4)/0.055|}\), landing in the 1–4 meV range for current oscillation data.

Why it matters: this is a rare falsifiable quintessence model — the dark-energy scale becomes a prediction from PMNS parameters, with \(\delta_{\rm CP}\) accounting for \(\sim80\%\) of the variance, so Hyper-K/DUNE directly test it. It also predicts \(m_{\beta\beta}\simeq1.5\)–3.7 meV (below next-gen reach) and behaves as instability-free thawing quintessence for \(f\sim M_{\rm pl}\). Caveats, stated frankly: it does not address the cosmological-constant problem (vacuum energy assumed zero), faces a “severe axion-quality problem” requiring \(|\Delta V_{\rm UV}|\lesssim\rho_{\rm DE}\), and needs near-Planckian \(f\) — treated as low-energy assumptions rather than derived. NuFit and long-baseline (T2K/NOvA) best-fits currently map to different \(\Lambda_\nu\).

arXiv:2607.13758 §

Evidence for sequential Υ(nS) suppression in light ion collisions

CMS Collaboration

CMS reports the first measurements of \(\Upsilon(1S,2S,3S)\) in oxygen-oxygen and \(\Upsilon(1S,2S)\) in neon-neon collisions at \(\sqrt{s_{NN}}=5.36\) TeV, with a \(pp\) reference at the same energy (integrated luminosities 8.4 nb\(^{-1}\), 0.81 nb\(^{-1}\), 456 pb\(^{-1}\)). Double ratios of excited-to-ground-state yields relative to \(pp\) come out below unity in OO: \(D_{21}=0.664\pm0.055\pm0.007\), \(D_{31}=0.392\pm0.077\pm0.023\), \(D_{32}=0.59\pm0.12\pm0.04\) — the last below unity by 3.2\(\sigma\), the headline “evidence” for sequential suppression in a light-ion system. In NeNe, \(D_{21}=0.27\pm0.12\pm0.01\), lower than OO by 3.0\(\sigma\). The OO \(\Upsilon(3S)/\Upsilon(2S)\) ratio is compatible with PbPb across \(p_T\) and integrated, supporting a common QGP-medium interpretation.

Why it matters: it extends state-dependent bottomonium suppression to \(\langle N_{\rm part}\rangle\approx10\), a system size an order of magnitude below PbPb, reinforcing that QGP-like final-state effects need not require large volumes — bearing on the long-running debate over deconfinement in small systems. Caveats the paper flags: the \(D_{32}\) significance is modest (3.2\(\sigma\)); event-activity trends “should be carefully interpreted” since multiplicity alone does not fix suppression (head-on OO can rival PbPb particle production per participant); and the shinchon transport model “significantly underestimates” the measured suppression, hinting at missing medium dynamics.

arXiv:2607.12879 §

Observation of pair production of longitudinally polarized Z bosons in four-lepton final state with the ATLAS detector

ATLAS Collaboration

(abstract only) ATLAS measures \(ZZ\) production in the \(4\ell\) channel (\(ee\)/\(\mu\mu\) pairs) using 164 fb\(^{-1}\) at \(\sqrt{s}=13.6\) TeV, extracting polarization fractions inclusively and in two \(m_{4\ell}\) regions — all consistent with the SM. The headline comes from combining with the earlier 13 TeV measurement: the first observation of two simultaneously longitudinally polarized Z bosons, at 6.5σ observed (5.6σ expected). This pushes the electroweak-symmetry-breaking program — long anchored in single-boson \(W_L\)/\(Z_L\) fractions and \(W_LW_L\) scattering — into the doubly-longitudinal diboson sector, directly probing the Goldstone-mode content of two gauge bosons at once. The caveat is structural: a genuine observation is only reached after statistical combination across two center-of-mass energies, so the 13.6 TeV dataset alone is not yet decisive, and the analysis reports agreement with the SM rather than any deviation.

arXiv:2607.12004 §

Bounds on axion-like particles from fully merged photons at the LHC

Simon Knapen

Knapen recasts the ATLAS 139 fb\(^{-1}\) high-mass diphoton resonance search (2102.13405) into an ALP-photon bound by exploiting that for \(0.1\)–\(6\) GeV ALPs the two decay photons from \(q\bar q\to a\gamma\) merge into a single reconstructed photon. Because the \(aF\tilde F\) operator is dimension-five, the parton cross section is energy-independent while the \(\gamma\gamma\) background falls as \(1/m_{\gamma\gamma}^2\), so the signal-to-background ratio grows as \((m_{\gamma\gamma}g_{a\gamma\gamma})^2\); a profile-likelihood fit to bins with \(m_{\gamma\gamma}>1.5\) TeV yields \(g_{a\gamma\gamma}\approx 4\times10^{-4}\) GeV\(^{-1}\), the strongest current bound over most of \(0.1\)–\(6\) GeV. PDF systematics are shown subdominant (~10% of the statistical uncertainty). This is a clean, reinterpretation-style result that plants an LHC flag in a mass window otherwise covered by beam dumps, FASER and \(e^+e^-\) machines — squarely today’s “axions fanning out” theme.

Honest caveats are flagged by the author himself: the limit weakens below 0.2 GeV (displaced decays) and above 1 GeV due to a deliberately conservative tight-photon merging requirement (\(\Delta\eta<0.003\)). A dedicated ATLAS analysis with one loose + one tight photon should do better. Also, under the alternative \(c_W\approx c_B\) ansatz the \(B\to Ka\) bound (\(g_{a\gamma\gamma}\lesssim5\times10^{-6}\) GeV\(^{-1}\)) is far stronger — so the result’s competitiveness is specific to the \(c_B\gg c_W\) scenario.

arXiv:2607.12033 §

Three-Loop QCD Corrections to Scattering Amplitudes of a Higgs Boson and Three Partons

Xin Guan et al.

Guan, Mistlberger and Ruf deliver the full-color three-loop helicity amplitudes for \(H+3\) partons (\(Hggg\) and \(Hq\bar qg\)) in the heavy-top EFT, using Blade IBP reduction via block-triangular forms and canonical differential equations, with results in compact generalized-polylogarithm form (full reduction ~several hundred thousand core-hours). All checks pass: infrared pole structure, collinear/soft factorization, AMFlow numerics, agreement with the prior leading-color result (2504.06490), and the striking confirmation that the leading-transcendental part coincides with the \(\mathcal N=4\) sYM \(\mathrm{tr}(\phi^2)\) form factor — now including subleading color. These are the virtual ingredients for \(H+\)jet at N\(^3\)LO and a building block toward inclusive gluon-fusion at N\(^4\)LO.

The notable phenomenological finding: at N\(^3\)LO the subleading-color contribution to the amplitude is of the same order as the leading-color term — in sharp contrast to lower loops, where subleading color is negligible. This is a direct caution against generalized-leading-color truncation at this order. The reassuring counterpoint is that, evaluated at \(\alpha_s=0.118\), the net three-loop impact on the tree-normalized interference is small (sub-percent shift in the cumulant on the \(t=u\) slice), so the series remains well-behaved even as its color decomposition becomes non-hierarchical.

arXiv:2607.10606 §

The Neutron Electric Dipole Moment from Lattice QCD using a Background Electric Field

Thomas Blum et al.

Blum et al. extract the θ̄-induced neutron EDM from the energy shift of a neutron in a static, imaginary-valued Euclidean electric field, arriving at \(d_n = -0.0050(4)_{\rm stat}(8)_{\rm sys}\,\bar\theta\,e\,\)fm at the physical point. Two methodological moves drive the precision. First, motivated by Feynman-Hellmann, they sample the topological charge density on a single time slice rather than integrating the global charge \(Q_{\rm top}\) — the disconnected topological correlator is the noise bottleneck, and localizing it plus a full-volume low-mode average sharpens the signal dramatically. Second, because the background field deforms the nucleon and mixes parity, the naive positive-parity projector suffers large excited-state contamination; they instead solve a non-Hermitian (PT-symmetric, \(S\)-pseudo-Hermitian) GEVP in the space of the nucleon, its parity partner and excitations to isolate the true ground state. Reassuringly, EDM values agree across chiral-covariant and non-covariant interpolators and across gluonic vs. pseudoscalar-density definitions of the topological charge, and the global-charge method gives results consistent with (but far noisier than) the new estimator.

This is the kind of controlled number the EDM and \(0\nu\beta\beta\) programs need to convert experimental limits into bounds on \(\bar\theta\) and BSM CP-violating operators, and the local-charge + GEVP toolkit extends naturally to the Weinberg and chromo-EDM operators. Caveats are stated frankly: the calculation uses a single lattice spacing (\(a\approx0.11\) fm) and pion masses of 340–576 MeV, so discretization, finite-volume and chiral-extrapolation systematics are deferred to future work; the electro-quenched approximation is assumed benign (~1% for magnetic moments). It also lands amid ongoing dispute over whether the θ-term generates a nonzero nEDM at all.

arXiv:2607.10272 §

On the Origins of the Strong CP Problem

Anthony G. Williams

Williams argues that the strong CP problem is contingent, not mandatory. He defines “minimal QCD” — the standard Lagrangian plus local gauge invariance, causal locality, and the functional integral — and shows that essentially every established nonperturbative result attributed to topology follows within it without assuming the gauge-field configuration space decomposes into integer-valued sectors. The topological susceptibility emerges as \(\chi=\langle\hat Q^2\rangle/V=\partial^2\varepsilon_0/\partial\theta^2\) with \(\theta\) treated purely as a source parameter for the local operator \(q(x)\); the axial anomaly identifies the invariant \(\bar\theta=\theta+\arg\det M\); and the ’t Hooft vertex, Witten–Veneziano (\(m_{\eta'}^2=2N_f\chi_{\rm YM}/f_0^2\)) and Leutwyler–Smilga (\(\chi=\Sigma/\sum_f m_f^{-1}\)) relations all rely only on local operators, semiclassical saddle points, or the large-\(N_c\) expansion. The integer-valued \(Q\in\mathbb{Z}\), the \(2\pi\)-periodicity, the sector weighting \(e^{i\theta Q}\), the physical θ-vacuum and hence the fine-tuning problem appear only after supplementing minimal QCD with smoothness/boundary assumptions (\(\pi_3(SU(3))=\mathbb Z\)) — assumptions, he stresses, that typical distributional (non-differentiable) functional-integral configurations need not satisfy, and that lattice topology recovers only via added constructions (gradient flow, index theorem).

If this framing holds, the “problem” becomes a question about which additional structure Nature actually requires, reframing the recent Nelson-Barr/PQ solution flurry as answers to a possibly-optional question. The honest caveat is baked in: this is a foundational/logical clarification, not a new prediction. Williams explicitly does not claim a θ-term is forbidden by symmetry, does not dispute the θ↔pseudoscalar-mass equivalence, and concedes future theory or data could yet demand the global structure — in which case the entire conventional literature immediately reapplies.

arXiv:2607.10595 §

Observation of new excited Σ_c⁰ states in B⁻→Λ_c⁺p̅π⁻

LHCb collaboration et al.

An LHCb amplitude analysis of \(B^-\to\Lambda_c^+\bar p\pi^-\) using the full 9 fb\(^{-1}\) Run 1+2 dataset (\(37.0\times10^3\) signal decays, 5.1% combinatorial background) resolves the \(\Lambda_c^+\pi^-\) spectrum into the known \(\Sigma_c(2455)^0\), \(\Sigma_c(2520)^0\) and \(\Sigma_c(2800)^0\) plus two new states, \(\Sigma_c(2900)^0\) and \(\Sigma_c(3200)^0\) — the first new excited \(\Sigma_c^0\) baryons in over two decades. The two-resonance description of the ~2.8 GeV region (splitting the old BaBar \(\Sigma_c(2800)^0\) into overlapping states) is favored at \(7.5\sigma\), and the \(\Sigma_c(3200)^0\) is observed at \(12.0\sigma\). Combining the two solution families, they measure \(m_{\Sigma_c(3200)^0}=3.186\pm0.006\pm0.015\) GeV/\(c^2\), \(\Gamma=0.133\pm0.018\pm0.046\) GeV. The \(\Sigma_c(2520)^0\) spin-parity is pinned to \(3/2^+\) (>6σ) and the \(\Sigma_c(2455)^0\) spin to \(1/2\). Candidate interpretations span 2S/1P for the \(\Sigma_c(2900)^0\) and 2P/3S for the \(\Sigma_c(3200)^0\).

This materially expands the sparse \(\Sigma_c\) sector — only three states were previously established — providing masses and widths to confront the crowded quark-model predictions for 2S/1D/2P λ- and ρ-mode excitations, part of today’s experiment-anchored spectroscopy cluster. The central honest caveat is that spin-parity cannot be unambiguously fixed: multiple \(J^P\) assignments give nearly degenerate likelihoods, yielding two solution groups (A and B) statistically indistinguishable (\(2\Delta\ln\mathcal L<1\)) yet giving very different masses, widths and fit fractions for the \(\Sigma_c(2800)^0\)/\(\Sigma_c(2900)^0\) pair — group A matches Belle’s \(\Sigma_c(2800)^0\) (which sees no \(\Sigma_c(2900)^0\)), group B does not. Only the \(\Sigma_c(3200)^0\) parameters are robust across solutions.

(The digest’s two other “worth a look” picks — arXiv:2607.10417 and arXiv:2607.09845 — were not included in today’s full-text batch, so no deep dive is possible here.)

Monday, July 13, 2026

arXiv:2607.08904 §

Revisiting bounds on neutrino dark matter interaction at spikes

Yasaman Farzan

The finding: The much-quoted “spike” bounds on neutrino–DM scattering — derived from the attenuation of AGN neutrino fluxes traversing the dense DM cusp around supermassive black holes — collapse for light vector mediators. Farzan’s point is kinematic: the cascade equation contains both a loss term (\(-\sigma\Phi\)) and a regeneration term (the down-scattered flux re-populating each energy bin). Previous analyses (Cline et al., Cline–Puel, Dixit–Mohlabeng–Razzaque) kept only the loss term, valid only if each collision removes a neutrino from the beam. But when \(m_{Z'}^2/(2m_{DM})\ll E_\nu\), the differential cross section is sharply peaked at \(E'_\nu\simeq E_\nu\) — scattering is nearly forward, energy loss negligible, and the two terms cancel. Defining an effective suppression factor \(f\), for \(\Phi\propto E_\nu^{-3.2}\) and \(m_{Z'}^2/m_{DM}=2\) GeV, Farzan finds \(2\times10^{-3}<f<10^{-2}\) across the 1.5–15 TeV NGC 1068 window — relaxing the “most stringent” bound by up to ~1000×.

Why it matters: Landing the same day as Abbaslu et al.’s paper deriving AGN flavor signals from this same scattering, it’s a clean cautionary counterpoint: “spike constraints” on \(\sigma_{\nu DM}\) carry hidden microphysics (mediator mass, symmetric vs. asymmetric DM, flavor structure). Farzan further notes symmetric DM can annihilate to \(\nu\bar\nu\), and flavor-selective couplings can suppress attenuation while still distorting flavor ratios — leaving room for IceCube-Gen2/KM3NeT to discover spike effects rather than merely exclude them. Caveat: the companion analysis establishing the flavor phenomenology is “work in progress,” so the positive-signal claims rest partly on unpublished results.

arXiv:2607.09273 §

Neutrino-induced hyperon final-state interactions as constraints on the in-medium hyperon potential

Jaroslaw Nowak

What they did: Nowak proposes that charged-current accelerator (anti)neutrinos on \(^{40}\)Ar create \(\Lambda,\Sigma\) hyperons inside the nucleus, so their final-state interactions become a terrestrial probe of the in-medium potentials \(U_Y(\rho)\) that drive the neutron-star “hyperon puzzle.” Using the internal StrangeMC generator, three observables respond monotonically to the potentials: the trapped-\(\Lambda\) fraction and escaping-\(\Lambda\) momentum (for \(U_\Lambda\)), the escaping-\(\Sigma\) momentum, and a kaon-vetoed FSI-\(\Sigma^+\) tag (a final state the weak vertex cannot make) for \(U_\Sigma\). A detector-level Fisher forecast gives \(\delta U_\Lambda\approx0.3\) MeV and \(\delta U_\Sigma\approx3\)–4 MeV at fixed low-density slope \(\gamma\). Fed through a GM1 relativistic mean field anchored to established depths, the same potentials yield \(M_{\max}=1.94\,M_\odot\) and \(\Lambda_{1.4}=1034\).

The honesty: The headline sub-MeV number is heavily caveated. Because hyperons are born below saturation, \(U_\Lambda(\rho_0)\) and \(\gamma\) are 99.8% anti-correlated; marginalizing over \(\gamma\) (which the data alone fix only to \(\delta\gamma\approx0.8\)) degrades \(\delta U_\Lambda\) to 5.6 MeV. Two further systematics — the transport prescription (−6 MeV) and \(YN\) cross sections (−5/+2 MeV) — push the realistic \(U_\Lambda\) budget to ~10 MeV. Worse, the \(YN\) uncertainty biases \(U_\Sigma\) by \(\mathcal{O}(150)\) MeV, not an artifact of the \(\Sigma^+\) tag but because the \(\Lambda\) spectrum itself is \(YN\)-sensitive. The propagated \(M_{\max}\) is explicitly an inference dominated by the external high-density prior, not a neutrino measurement; the ~\(0.3\,M_\odot\) EOS-model systematic dwarfs the statistical reach. StrangeMC is uncommissioned against community generators — a stated next step.

arXiv:2607.09539 §

Top-Antitop Production and Decay at Threshold at the LHC in QCD Perturbation Theory

Paolo Nason et al.

What they did: Nason, Pelliccioli, Re and Rottoli deliver three NLO+PS generators (thr1, thr2, bb4l) incorporating threshold-enhanced (Coulomb/Sommerfeld) corrections in \(t\bar t\) production, extending their earlier NRR work up to \((\alpha_s/v)^6\) and, crucially, with finite-width treatment (thr2 modifies the phase space for off-shell tops; bb4l uses the full b_bbar_4l off-shell code). In the “ATLAS bin” (\(m_{t\bar t}<350\) GeV, \(p^\star<50\) GeV) they find total cross sections near 10 pb with a threshold excess over baseline NLO of ~4 pb — compatible with the Garzelli et al. NRQCD result and with the 8.5–9.3 pb “\(\eta_t\)” numbers CMS/ATLAS extract. The perturbative series converges: truncating at \(\alpha_s^3\) already captures the full result to better than 1 pb, so all-orders GFRW resummation is unnecessary at LHC resolution.

Why it matters: This directly addresses the recent near-threshold “toponium” observation, and sharpens the interpretation. The authors argue forcefully that (i) the pseudoscalar dominance follows from the Landau–Yang theorem for gluon fusion regardless of bound-state formation; (ii) toponium is part of the perturbative QCD cross section, so claiming an “excess above QCD consistent with QCD-predicted toponium” is contradictory; and (iii) the genuine bound-state contribution is small (~2.6–4.5 pb depending on generator/scale) and cannot be cleanly separated from continuum given the top width smearing. A new physical effect: imposing double-top virtuality cuts suppresses the sub-threshold cross section via phase-space reduction, cutting the bound-state yield by ~1 pb — captured by thr2/bb4l but not thr1. Caveat: the exact finite-width resummation formula (Appendix A) is derived but not implemented; single-top contamination near threshold requires a full experimental signal/background study the authors defer.

arXiv:2607.08552 §

The 20 GeV Galactic Halo Excess: Pixel-Level Confirmation and Consistency with Sub-TeV WIMP Annihilation

Trinity Rosebud Stenhouse et al.

Stenhouse, Ghag & Deppisch independently reproduce Totani’s cell-aggregated Fermi-LAT halo excess and extend it to a pixel-level Poisson likelihood on native 0.125° maps with energy-dependent PSF forward-folding and bright-source masking. The two methods agree on the spectrum — a peak at ~20.8 GeV — with the pixel-level normalization ~20% higher for the ρ² halo, confirming the feature is not an artifact of 10° binning. The excess is centrally concentrated (angular profile χ²/ν≃5–7 for NFW vs 30 for isotropic), spectrally and spatially distinct from the few-GeV GCE, and stable under a foreground-systematics suite and an injection-null test (a distorted-ICS mock with no halo yields ≤10% of the observed peak). Prompt s-wave fits give mχ≃0.55 TeV (W⁺W⁻) and 0.72 TeV (bb̄) with ⟨σv⟩≃1×10⁻²⁴ cm³ s⁻¹.

Why it matters: this is exactly the independent stress-test the weekly memory flagged. The headline tension is with dwarf-spheroidal limits, ℛ≃5.1 face-value, widening to 1.6–9.3 once foreground choice (GALPROP vs GIEM dominates, halving the flux) and J-factor uncertainties are folded in — so s-wave is not excluded but not comfortable. Crucially, p/d-wave over-resolves the dwarf tension but misses relic abundance by ~7 orders of magnitude; decay is disfavored by the IGRB. Only resonant Sommerfeld/Breit-Wigner survives, supplying the needed ≈45× boost — but requiring a fine-tuned above-threshold resonance and a structured dark sector. Caveat honestly stated: χ²/ν∼8 from coherent diffuse residuals, and AMS-02 antiprotons independently disfavor direct bb̄; this is “consistency,” not detection.

arXiv:2607.08587 §

A Novel Hadronic Calorimeter With A Direct Neutron Readout

I. Giomataris et al.

Giomataris et al. simulate (Geant4, benchmarked against FLUKA) a sampling calorimeter of alternating Pb absorbers and Gd-loaded LAB scintillator that reads out both the prompt scintillation and a delayed signal from neutron moderation and capture. In a six-layer prototype (3.66 λ_I) hit by 10 GeV protons, ~197 neutrons/proton are captured with a median capture time of 11.1 μs; the delayed deposited energy is almost perfectly proportional to capture multiplicity (R²=0.9998, slope 7.97 MeV/neutron), giving direct calibration. Gadolinium’s key benefit is timing, not yield: 0.5% Gd collects >85% of captures within 20 μs versus ~10% for pure LAB. Because neutron production tracks the invisible hadronic energy, a branch-wise event-by-event correction using the delayed signal improves prompt resolution from 21.8% to 13.3% — a ~63% variance reduction — with no event rejection.

Why it matters: this is a hardware attack on the invisible-energy fluctuations that fundamentally limit hadronic resolution, complementary to dual-readout. The intrinsic neutron-counting resolution (~7%) is far below the 21.8% calorimetric resolution, confirming that neutron-production fluctuations, not the detector, dominate — and can be measured out. Events binned by fixed neutron multiplicity show resolution falling to ~6%, suggesting the method scales favorably to higher energies. Caveats are explicit: a single 10 GeV energy, a shallow preliminary geometry, and no optical-photon transport, photosensor, or electronics modeling yet — the 13.3% is a proof-of-principle, not a device spec.

arXiv:2607.08175 §

Transformer-based machine learning using low-level calorimeter signals for collimated photon identification

Gabriel Matos et al.

(Abstract only.) The authors use calorimeter cell-level information to separate highly collimated diphoton signatures — from light axion-like-particle decays — from isolated single photons, systematically comparing six architectures spanning high-level shower-shape approaches and direct cell-level methods. Cell-level ML decisively outperforms shower-shape variables, with a Transformer best overall and an MLP Mixer offering a resource-constrained option for trigger-level deployment. Beyond classification, the Transformer performs direct invariant-mass regression from cells, sharpening light-resonance characterization and adding a handle to suppress π⁰ and η fake-photon backgrounds.

Why it matters: this is the calorimetry front of the week’s ML-reconstruction arc — the same raw-input-beats-engineered-features lesson seen in flavor tagging, now applied to fine-grained EM showers. Direct mass regression from cells is the notable step beyond classification. No numerical performance figures are available in the provided text.

arXiv:2607.07664 §

Single inclusive hadron and jet production in lepton-hadron scattering

Jian-Wei Qiu et al.

The first calculation of high-\(p_T\) single-inclusive hadron (\(\pi^\pm,K^\pm\)) and jet production in \(e+h\to H+X\) within joint QCD+QED collinear factorization, treating collision-induced QED radiation on equal footing with QCD. The cross section factorizes into hard parts (kept at NLO) convolved with universal lepton distribution functions (LDFs), PDFs (JAM20/CT18) and FFs (JAM20/MAP1.0), with DGLAP kernels carrying mixed QCD+QED splitting functions. Because a photon can split to \(q\bar q\) nonperturbatively, LDFs are genuinely nonperturbative objects; the authors start evolution at \(\mu_0=m_c\) (not \(m_e\)) and build a “default” LDF set from a Beta-function ansatz constrained by number and momentum sum rules, then evolve to LHAPDF6-compatible grids. Headline numbers: collision-induced QED radiation reshapes the hadron \(p_T\) spectrum by up to ~50% (depleting high-\(p_T\), enhancing low-\(p_T\)); jet spectra drop ~10–20%; scale uncertainty runs ~20–25%, but the LDF (\(\mu_e\)) piece is only a few percent. Nuclear modification factors show EMC suppression below \(p_T\sim10\) GeV and Fermi-motion enhancement above.

Why it matters: this is the LDF program’s most concrete deliverable — a unified treatment of leptoproduction and photoproduction with no ad hoc \(Q^2\) cut or “resolved photon” structure, exactly the framework the digest flags as sub-percent-precision machinery aimed at JLab and the EIC. The key caveat is honest and stated repeatedly: the LDFs are still model defaults (quark/gluon content set to zero at input, so those are lower limits), and existing data carry traditional radiative corrections and cannot be used to fit true LDFs until reanalyzed à la ZEUS. Dominant theory uncertainty is currently the FF choice, not the scales.

arXiv:2607.07800 §

Search for axion-like particles decaying to two photons at Belle II

Belle II Collaboration et al.

Using 408 fb\(^{-1}\) of Belle II data, a search for \(e^+e^-\to\gamma a\), \(a\to\gamma\gamma\) reconstructs the ALP as a narrow diphoton peak over \(0.17<m_a<9.80\) GeV/\(c^2\), assuming photon-dominant coupling (\(\mathcal{B}(a\to\gamma\gamma)=1\)). Three-photon events are selected with a helicity-angle cut (\(|\cos\lambda|<0.74\)) to suppress the dominant \(\gamma\gamma(\gamma)\) background, a kinematic fit sharpening the mass resolution to 10–16 MeV/\(c^2\), and a 19-feature neural-network classifier. No significant excess: the largest local significance is \(3.3\sigma\) near \(m_a=0.22\) GeV/\(c^2\), reducing to \(1.4\sigma\) global after look-elsewhere. The resulting 95% CL limits on \(g_{a\gamma\gamma}\) reach \(\sim10^{-4}\) GeV\(^{-1}\) and are the most restrictive to date over nearly all of \(0.17<m_a<5.00\) GeV/\(c^2\) (except near 0.22 GeV/\(c^2\)), improving on the prior Belle II result by up to a factor 9.

Why it matters: this cleanly extends the dark-sector portal coverage flagged in today’s digest, closing an intermediate-mass gap between beam-dump/forward limits and heavy-ion light-by-light constraints with a single tabletop-clean channel. Improvements over the 2020 Belle II analysis come from the kinematic fit, the NN, and the larger sample. Caveats are modest and disclosed: the coupling extraction ignores possible energy dependence of \(\alpha_\text{QED}\) and \(g_{a\gamma\gamma}\), and \(\eta/\omega\) mass windows are vetoed for peaking backgrounds. The mild \(0.22\) GeV/\(c^2\) bump is statistically unremarkable.

arXiv:2607.07969 §

Experimental access to the gluonic origin of the proton mass

Zein-Eddine Meziani et al.

A concrete reconstruction scheme for the scalar gluonic trace form factor \(G_N(t)\) — the dominant, trace-anomaly source of the proton mass — from three measurable inputs. Their central identity (Eq. 14) writes \(G_N(t)=G_{s,g}(t)+G_{s,q}(t)-\sigma(t)/M\): the quark scalar gravitational form factor from DVCS, the gluon scalar GFF from near-threshold \(J/\psi\) photoproduction (extracted via holographic QCD from JLab data), and the nucleon sigma-term form factor. At zero momentum transfer this reduces to \(G_N(0)=1-\sigma(0)/M_N\). Plugging in the phenomenological \(A_q(0)=0.576\pm0.011\) and lattice dipole/tripole GFF fits, the reconstructed \(G_N(t)\) falls rapidly with \(-t\) and agrees, within bands, with an independent lattice-QCD trace-anomaly determination — and, for \(-t\lesssim1\) GeV\(^2\), with the interacting instanton liquid model, supporting a picture where the long-distance scalar gluonic structure is dominated by semiclassical topological glue.

Why it matters: this converts an abstract mass-decomposition question into a defined experimental program, tying together DVCS, near-threshold quarkonium, and \(\sigma\)-term measurements — exactly the gravitational-form-factor thread the digest has tracked this week. The lattice consistency is a genuine cross-check rather than an input. Caveats: the result relies on holographic-QCD modeling to extract \(G_{s,g}\) from \(J/\psi\) data and on lattice/phenomenological fits for the quark piece, so it is a proof-of-principle reconstruction rather than a fully data-driven extraction; ILM–lattice agreement degrades above \(-t\simeq1\) GeV\(^2\) where short-distance gluon dynamics enter.

Tuesday, July 7, 2026

arXiv:2607.06108 §

Non-Unitarity Effects and Fake CP Violation in Neutrino Oscillation Experiments

Pratima Singh et al.

Using GLoBES with a custom non-unitary probability engine, Singh et al. quantify how a single off-diagonal PMNS deviation \(\alpha_{21}=|\alpha_{21}|e^{i\varphi_{21}}\) fakes CP violation at DUNE and Hyper-K. The headline: at each experiment’s oscillation maximum the fake asymmetry is a mild perturbation (1.7% of the genuine swing at DUNE’s 2.4 GeV, 4.5% at Hyper-K’s 0.5 GeV for \(|\alpha_{21}|=0.01\)), but in suppressed-genuine windows near 1.5–1.6 GeV the fake-to-genuine ratio exceeds unity — 129% at DUNE, 134% at Hyper-K, with a second 129% window for Hyper-K at 2.0 GeV. In these bins an \(A_{CP}\)-only analysis cannot separate real from fake CP, and the sign of \(\varphi_{21}\) is invisible in the broad-peak region. The degeneracy analysis shows Hyper-K alone leaves \(\varphi_{21}\) essentially unconstrained at \(\ge2\sigma\) (matter effects too weak to reject the wrong ordering, \(\chi^2_{\rm min}=4.4\)), while DUNE’s 1300 km matter lever arm pins it (\(\chi^2_{\rm min}=486\)). Their combination shrinks the \(3\sigma\) allowed \((\delta_{CP},\varphi_{21})\) area to \(0.059\pi^2\) — 16.3× smaller than Hyper-K alone.

Why it matters: this is the week’s reference point for “fake CP.” It concretely localizes where non-unitarity is dangerous (specific energy bins, not the oscillation maximum) and confirms the recurring prescription — combine complementary baselines rather than trusting either detector. Caveat: only the minimal single-parameter \(\alpha_{21}=0.01\) scenario is explored, and the combined \(3\sigma\) area is marginally larger than DUNE alone, an honestly-flagged artifact of Hyper-K’s flat directions overlapping DUNE’s contour edge.

arXiv:2607.06862 §

Probing Quasi-Dirac Neutrino Oscillations at Long Baseline Experiments

Noor-Ines Boudjema et al.

Boudjema, Deppisch & Pattanaik embed the quasi-Dirac scenario — small lepton-number-violating Majorana mass \(\mu\) splitting a near-degenerate sterile pair — in an inverse-seesaw-motivated \(3+2\) framework, deriving analytic \(\nu_\mu\to\nu_e\) and \(\nu_\mu\to\nu_\mu\) probabilities with the sterile splitting \(\Delta m^2_{54}\propto\mu\) driving a slow interference term. Simulating NOvA, T2K and DUNE in GLoBES with full systematics, they map sensitivity in \((\theta_{sa},\Delta m^2_{54})\). Current NOvA and T2K each reach \(\theta_{sa}\approx0.1\); combined, \(\approx0.08\). DUNE alone dominates, probing \(\theta_{sa}\approx0.05\) — roughly a factor-two improvement — with approximately uniform coverage across \(\Delta m^2_{54}\) because its long baseline averages out the rapid sterile oscillations. NOvA/T2K instead show a resonance enhancement near \(\Delta m^2_{54}\sim\Delta m^2_{31}\sim2.4\times10^{-3}\) eV², plus degeneracy valleys at \(\theta_{sa}\approx\theta_{23}\approx0.4\) where the \(5\nu\) model mimics \(3\nu\).

Why it matters: this adds quasi-Dirac splittings to the week’s “what mimics CP” catalogue, now constrained with real NOvA/T2K data rather than forecasts alone. A key structural result is that quasi-Dirac sensitivity is intrinsically coupled to \(\delta_{CP}\): the \(5\nu\) appearance probability carries a \(c_{sa}\) suppression factor modulating the CP-dependent amplitude, so DUNE’s quasi-Dirac and CP programs necessarily inform one another. Caveats: flavour-universal sterile mixing (\(\theta_{sa}\) common to all angles) and vanishing sterile CP phases are assumed throughout — both acknowledged as simplifications that could resolve or deepen the identified degeneracies. Current NOvA/T2K cannot exclude the scenario across benchmark phases and orderings.

arXiv:2607.06059 §

A resonance-aware MC@NLO QCD+EW-matched calculation of lepton-pair production

Lois Flower et al.

Flower, Roper & Schönherr present the first automated MC@NLO matching of NLO QCD+EW to an interleaved QCD+QED parton shower, built on Catani-Seymour dipoles in SHERPA. The core technical contribution is a resonance-aware modification: dipoles spanning a colour/charge-neutral \(s\)-channel resonance are switched off (via \(\Theta\)-functions on emission scale \(t_{\rm res}\sim\Gamma_r^2\) and virtuality measure \(\Delta_r\)) when an emission is hard enough to resolve it, preventing spurious recoil-induced distortions of the resonance lineshape. They also identify that standard interleaved matching leaves uncancelled \(\mathcal{O}(\alpha_s\alpha)\) terms — the QCD Sudakov \(\bar\Delta\ll1\) multiplying QED dipoles drives the photon \(p_\perp\) spectrum negative over 15–40 GeV — and cure it with an extra physically-motivated Sudakov on the H-events (dubbed MC@NLO\(^{\rm sud}\)), at the cost of a formally higher-order shift to the inclusive cross section.

Validated against fixed-order NLO QCD+EW and against MC@NLO QCD+YFS, the method gives, for \(pp\to e^+e^-\), a resonance-aware distortion of the dressed dilepton mass of a few per mille (0.2% below the \(Z\) peak, 0.5% at \(m_{ee}\approx60\) GeV) and a percent-level change in hard-photon \(p_\perp\), rising to +15% at \(p_{\perp,\gamma}\approx1\) TeV from missing ISR-FSR interference. Why it matters: Drell-Yan underpins calibration, PDF extraction and normalization, so this is likely to become standard HL-LHC precision machinery. Caveat: the resonance-aware implementation is currently process-specific (though the algorithm is general), and colour/charged resonances need new emitter/spectator splitting functions left to future work.

Monday, July 6, 2026

arXiv:2607.05145 §

Search for nonresonant triple Higgs boson production in the six-b-quark final state at √s = 13 TeV

CMS Collaboration

Using the full Run 2 dataset (138 fb⁻¹), CMS performs the first nonresonant \(HHH\to6b\) search built around an inclusive transformer/SPANet reconstruction that spans all resolved-to-merged topologies (three \(H\) from small-radius jet pairs, from large-radius jets, or mixtures). A two-stage SPANet classifier plus a data-driven QCD-multijet model (95% of background) with \(b/bb\)-tag score resampling yields no excess. The observed (expected) 95% CL upper limit is 44 (43) fb, or 588 (572)× the SM \(\sigma^{HHH}_{\rm ggF}=0.08\) fb — the most stringent triple-Higgs bound to date. In the coupling framework, \(-7.4<\kappa_3<12.4\) (for \(\kappa_4=1\)) and \(-177<\kappa_4<185\) (for \(\kappa_3=1\)), improving on ATLAS’s earlier \(6b\) result (\(-11<\kappa_3<17\); \(-230<\kappa_4<240\)).

Why it matters: the two-parameter \((\kappa_3,\kappa_4)\) scan, for the first time in a direct \(HHH\) search, excludes a slice of parameter space previously bounded only by perturbative unitarity — pushing the quartic-coupling frontier from theory into data, as the digest flags. Sensitivity is limited by data statistics (~52% of the expected limit), with the QCD-multijet shape the leading systematic. Honest caveats: the coupling scans assume SM signal topology at every coupling value (cross-section-only scaling), and \(\kappa_4\)-dependent kinematic/shape effects in \(HH\) are deferred; including realistic kinematics degrades the expected \(\kappa_3\) bound by 12–15%. The \(HH\) contribution is fixed to \(\mu_{HH}=1\) with profiled uncertainty. HL-LHC projections (S3 scenario) are provided but the current constraint remains ~600× SM.

arXiv:2607.05322 §

b-jet identification efficiency for GN2 in dileptonic tt̄ at √s = 13.6 TeV

The ATLAS Collaboration

(Abstract only.) ATLAS calibrates the transformer-based GN2 flavour tagger on 56 fb⁻¹ of 2022–2023 Run 3 data, extracting the \(b\)-jet efficiency and jet-flavour composition simultaneously from a \(t\bar t\)-enriched dileptonic sample, binned in jet \(p_T\) (20–400 GeV) across six cumulative-efficiency working points. GN2 delivers up to 2× (3×) higher light-flavour (charm) rejection than its predecessor DL1d at fixed \(b\)-efficiency. Data/simulation correction factors fall in 0.9–1.3, with total uncertainty around 1% for \(p_T>60\) GeV in the loosest ([65%,0%]) interval.

Why it matters: this converts GN2 from demonstrator to calibrated production tool on Run 3 data, backing the digest’s “transformer wave” theme with benchmarked numbers — the same tagging performance that underpins searches like the \(HHH\to6b\) analysis above. The ~1% high-\(p_T\) uncertainty sets the achievable precision for the many Run 3 analyses that will now adopt GN2.

arXiv:2607.05514 §

Deuterium-Proton Fusion in an EFT Constructed from On-Shell Amplitudes

Tim M. P. Tait

Tait builds \(d(p,\gamma)^3\)He radiative capture as a nuclear-state EFT in which deuteron, proton, and \(^3\)He are point-like degrees of freedom, and constructs the amplitude entirely from on-shell methods — massive spinor-helicity three-point vertices, BCFW recursion, and an enumerated tower of transverse boundary (two-body-current) operators — bypassing any Lagrangian. The expansion parameter is \(p_{\rm rel}/p_*\) with \(p_*\approx53\) MeV set by deuteron breakup, giving a contact-operator scale \(\Lambda=\sqrt{4\pi}\,p_*\approx187\) MeV. A global Bayesian fit to LUNA/Türkat capture data plus \(^3\)He ANC and NDA priors returns \(S(0)=0.209\pm0.008\) eV b (between Solar Fusion III’s 0.203 and Marcucci’s ab initio 0.216), with \(\chi^2/{\rm dof}=0.83\).

The headline physics result: the persistent offset between the ab initio benchmark and the sub-percent LUNA data is traced to a single natural-sized next-to-leading \(E1\) contact term, \(t_{E1}\simeq-0.146\pm0.021\) (~15% amplitude, ~30% rate deficit), degenerate with a ~15%-lower effective \(^3\)He asymptotic normalization. This recasts the PRIMAT–PArthENoPE split (a ~2σ \(D/H\) discrepancy) from a choice between curves into a measurable low-energy constant. Caveats stated plainly: the angle-integrated \(S\)-factor cannot separate the contact term from the ANC (a precise \(p\)–\(d\) doublet elastic measurement is proposed), Türkat data sit ~2.3σ high and are floated, quadrupole contacts are prior-limited, and the analytic work was AI-assisted (Claude), human-validated.

Sunday, July 5, 2026

arXiv:2607.04067 §

GPU-Accelerated Matrix-Based Hough Transform for Online Track Reconstruction in the STCF MDC

Liang Peng et al.

The authors propose a GPU-accelerated Hough transform for track reconstruction in the STCF Main Drift Chamber (MDC), specifically targeting low-transverse-momentum particles. The method reformulates the conventional Hough transform by applying a conformal transformation to map circular trajectories into linear features, followed by a transition from serial parameter-space voting to a regularized, matrix-based parallel computational flow. The architecture is co-designed to align with CUDA thread structures, partitioning the Hough parameter space into angular columns to minimize atomic-operation contention and maximize memory coalescing.

The headline result is a 151.57x speedup over a conventional CPU implementation, achieving end-to-end processing of 1,000 events in approximately 0.14 seconds. Performance evaluation on five simulated physics channels shows a signal retention ratio of 93.04% while reducing the hit volume to 34.92% of its original size. The primary caveat is that the algorithm’s performance degrades for secondary-vertex tracks with large impact parameters (\(d_0\)), as the current Hough parameterization is optimized for tracks originating near the primary vertex. The authors note that addressing these complex topologies will require future integration with Kalman filtering for iterative hit association and parameter correction.

arXiv:2607.04271 §

Neural-Network-Assisted Binary Template Construction for Matrix-Based Pattern Matching in the STCF MDC

Zhaoli Guo et al.

This paper introduces a framework to optimize the binary template libraries used for MDC local segment finding, moving beyond manual rule-based construction. The authors employ a neural network as an offline differentiable optimization tool to learn template parameters—specifically “trigger” (core skeleton) and “recovery” (envelope) templates—by minimizing a multi-objective loss function that balances signal retention, noise rejection, and template redundancy. Crucially, the neural network is used only for training; the resulting binary templates are exported for use in a standard bitwise pattern-matching routine, preserving deterministic and low-latency online execution.

The framework successfully covers the \(p_T > 120\) MeV/c range, maintaining high signal retention even under background conditions up to 3x the nominal rate. Throughput reaches ~60 kHz per GPU, which scales to >200 kHz on multi-GPU systems. A notable caveat is the observed trade-off between momentum coverage and output fraction: extending coverage to the lowest momentum tracks (where local segments become highly inclined) increases the output fraction, as these templates become sparser and more susceptible to background triggers. The authors suggest that for very low-momentum particles that do not fully traverse the MDC, this template-based finding should be treated as a preselection step for global track-finding algorithms rather than a standalone reconstruction solution.

arXiv:2607.04136 §

Naturally Light Composite Higgs as a Protected Collective Eigenmode

Gauhar Abbas

This paper defines a new class of light composite Higgs models where the Higgs is protected as an “eigenvalue” of a strongly coupled scalar kernel rather than by a coset shift symmetry or accidental criticality. The author provides three basis-invariant diagnostics to classify light scalars: the sector participation number (\(P_{\rm sec}\)), the sector gap ratio (\(R_{\rm coll}\)), and the microscopic sensitivity (\(\Delta_g\)). A Higgs is “protected” if \(R_{\rm coll} \gg 1\), \(P_{\rm sec} > 1\), and \(\Delta_g = \mathcal{O}(1)\). The author demonstrates this mechanism using a Dark Technicolor (DTC) topology where a rank-one locking invariant forbids tree-level curvature, and a universal DQCD bridge suppresses one-loop hard curvature, leading to \(\Delta_g = 2\).

The theory offers a falsifiable path via sector-restricted GEVP lattice spectroscopy, where the sensitivity of the mass to coupling variations can be scanned. The author notes that while the scalar protection is structural, the model remains subject to the oblique \(S\) parameter constraints; these are addressed here via a hidden-local link mechanism that effectively decouples technicolor vectors from the electroweak vacuum. The primary caveat is the dependence on the assumption of a universal DQCD-generated constituent mass for bridge fermions; non-universality in the DQCD bridge immediately regenerates hard sensitivity, shifting the model back into the “accidental cancellation” category.

Saturday, July 4, 2026

arXiv:2607.03840 §

Neutron stars with an agnostic Dark sector: Core and Halo configurations from a two-fluid approach

Asit karan et al.

The authors employ a two-fluid Tolman–Oppenheimer–Volkoff (TOV) framework to investigate dark-matter-admixed neutron stars (DMANSs) without assuming specific dark sector microphysics. By combining chiral effective field theory (low density) and perturbative QCD (high density) for nuclear matter with a speed-of-sound interpolation scheme for both nuclear and dark fluids, they generate \(\sim 10^5\) mass-radius sequences. They find that light dark matter (\(m_D \lesssim 0.3\) GeV) preferentially forms extended halos that increase tidal deformability, while heavy dark matter (\(m_D \gtrsim 0.7\) GeV) forms compact cores that enhance compactness.

This work marks a significant shift toward “dark sector agnosticism,” replacing model-specific assumptions with thermodynamically consistent speed-of-sound profiles. By confronting these configurations with GW170817 tidal bounds and NICER mass-radius data, they establish robust constraints—most notably that the dark matter fraction \(f_{\mathrm{DM}} \lesssim 0.11\) for light models. The analysis demonstrates that the dominant observational diagnostic shifts from gravitational-wave tidal deformability (for halos) to NICER mass-radius measurements (for cores), providing a clear roadmap for future multi-messenger observations.

The caveat is that their agnostic framework relies on the assumption that the dark matter is well-described by a free Fermi gas at low density (\(n < 0.1 n_0\)) before the speed-of-sound interpolation begins. While this provides a conservative boundary, it excludes more exotic, non-degenerate, or strongly interacting dark sector candidates.

arXiv:2607.03716 §

Relativistic Vorticity in the Quark-Gluon Plasma: Generation Symmetries, Explosive Dilution, and Azimuthal Spin Alignment

Malak Ait Tamlihat et al.

Note: Only the abstract was available.

The authors argue that the intense longitudinal and radial expansion of the Quark-Gluon Plasma (QGP) at RHIC and LHC acts as a “geometric shield” that regularizes vortex dynamics. They show that this explosive volumetric dilatation suppresses vortex stretching, naturally limiting the global mid-rapidity hyperon polarization to \(P_\Lambda \lesssim 10^{-4}\). This mechanism provides a hydrodynamic explanation for why observational signals of rotation in the plasma are significantly smaller than earlier, less-refined fluid models predicted.

This result is a crucial refinement for the heavy-ion community, suggesting that the “missing” rotation signature is not necessarily an indication of physics beyond the Standard Model but a consequence of the QGP’s own extreme expansion. The authors assert that the true signal of plasma rotation must be extracted via more subtle azimuthal differential measurements rather than global polarization observables. The limited dataset constraint is inherent to the theoretical nature of this study, as it relies on covariant transport equations rather than direct experimental re-analysis.

arXiv:2607.03885 §

Identifying lensed gravitational waves with physics-informed posterior learning

Tian-Yang Sun et al.

The authors introduce a “physics-informed posterior learning” framework to rank candidate lensed gravitational wave signals against unrelated multiple-merger events. By training a neural posterior estimator (NPE) on the common-source chirp mass and symmetric mass ratio—the fundamental parameters preserved by lensing—they force the classifier to prioritize events consistent with a single binary origin. This fusion of waveform morphology with posterior-based source consistency improves detection efficiency from 20.8% to 35.2% at a 1% false-positive rate.

This method exemplifies the trend of using machine learning to enforce physical symmetries rather than pattern-matching blips in the data. By separating the lens-family-agnostic training from held-out evaluations (PM, SIS, SIE, and SIE+Shear), they demonstrate that their classifier generalizes across diverse gravitational lens topologies. They report an SNR-equivalent distance scale increase of 1.35, suggesting that this approach significantly hardens search sensitivity against the “noise” of overlapping unrelated merger events.

A notable caveat is that the method’s performance depends heavily on the simulated unrelated multiple-merger population used for training. Because the rare-event tail is dominated by loud, temporally compact, and partly source-consistent unrelated signals, the results are sensitive to the assumed merger rate and mass distribution, as evidenced by the performance degradation when evaluated on populations outside the training prior.

Friday, July 3, 2026

arXiv:2607.03123 §

Polarized Nucleon as a Topological Dipole

Kenji Fukushima et al.

Fukushima and Uji propose that a polarized nucleon exhibits a non-trivial, dipole-like distribution of topological charge density, rather than a spherically symmetric one. By mapping the nucleon matrix element of the topological charge density (\(q_{\text{top}}\)) to the flavor-singlet axial charge (\(\Delta\Sigma\)), they derive a robust, model-independent dipole structure proportional to \(\boldsymbol{S} \cdot \hat{\boldsymbol{r}}\). This topological dipole moment is explicitly demonstrated using a chiral soliton model with \(U(1)_A\) anomaly.

This result bridges deep non-perturbative QCD topology with measurable hadronic structure. If accurate, this topological dipole provides a mechanism for local parity-odd topological charge to manifest in observable azimuthal asymmetries during relativistic heavy-ion collisions—specifically through directed-flow-like patterns in \(\eta\) and \(\eta^\prime\) meson production. Unlike the conventional Chiral Magnetic Effect, which relies on event-by-event fluctuations of random signs, this prediction suggests a persistent, spin-correlated signal that could be isolated in future exclusive production experiments. The authors acknowledge that isolating these anomaly-induced contributions from background transverse-photon amplitudes remains a significant experimental hurdle.

arXiv:2607.03370 §

The running of the electroweak gauge couplings from first principles

Alessandro Conigli et al.

Conigli et al. present a high-precision lattice QCD determination of the hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contribution to the running of the electromagnetic coupling (\(\alpha\)) and the weak mixing angle (\(\sin^2\theta_W\)). By using 27 CLS lattice ensembles with pion masses at the physical point, they achieve a total relative uncertainty of \(0.17\%\) for \(\Delta\alpha_{\text{had}}^{(5)}(M_Z^2) = 0.027821(34)_{\text{lat}}(35)_{\text{pQCD}}\), doubling the precision of current phenomenological estimates.

The headline finding is a \(7\sigma\) discrepancy at \(Q^2 \simeq 1 \text{ GeV}^2\) between their lattice results and traditional dispersive estimates derived from \(e^+e^- \to \text{hadrons}\) data. This tension is a critical sanity check for the field, as the HVP contribution is a leading uncertainty in SM precision tests. While the tension diminishes at the \(Z\)-pole due to perturbative running, the result signals that “first-principles” lattice infrastructure is now outperforming dispersive fits. The authors provide a quantitative roadmap for future colliders (like FCC-ee), showing that reaching the \(3 \times 10^{-5}\) target precision requires a \(50\%\) reduction in lattice uncertainty combined with an increased Euclidean matching scale (\(Q_0^2 \sim 20 \text{ GeV}^2\)).

arXiv:2607.03153 §

RTL Fault Injection of a Deployed Graph Neural Network Trigger for Belle II

Georgios Sotiropoulos et al.

Sotiropoulos et al. conducted an RTL-level fault injection study on the GNN-ETM, the Level-1 trigger for the Belle II electromagnetic calorimeter, injecting over 1.4 million Single-Event Upsets (SEUs). They targeted three critical failure modes: deadlocks, timeouts, and packet-integrity violations. The study highlights a “monitoring asymmetry” in current trigger verification: deadlocks inside the GNN accelerator are caught by internal watchdogs, while faults in other pipeline stages (Preprocessing, Postprocessing, Belle2Link) are only detected at the final output, leading to significant detection latency.

By implementing inter-stage liveness monitoring at the AXI-Stream interfaces, the authors demonstrate that standard “output-only” observation inflates Mean Time To Failure (MTTF) estimates by up to \(78.7\%\). This result underscores that as triggers shift toward complex GNN-based pipelines, traditional end-to-end monitoring is insufficient for high-reliability environments. The study provides a clear prioritization for hardware hardening: the Preprocessing and GNN Dataflow stages are identified as the most sensitive to radiation, serving as a template for future L1 trigger design.

arXiv:2607.03560 §

Black Hole Memory Burden and its Signatures in Gravitational Waves from Mergers

Gia Dvali et al.

This pick is based on the abstract, which explores the link between the information-theoretic “memory burden” of black holes and detectable gravitational-wave signatures.

Dvali et al. propose that black holes possess an “information memory burden” related to the microstate configuration of their horizon, which manifests as a subtle, time-dependent shift in the gravitational-wave (GW) signal during the merger process. By treating the black hole as a saturated state of gravitons, they argue that the emission process must account for the information content of the horizon, leading to specific corrections in the waveform phase that could be detectable by next-generation LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA sensitivity levels.

This paper addresses the long-standing “information paradox” through the lens of observable phenomenology. If the memory burden is significant, it effectively introduces a “de-coherence” or frequency-dependent drift in the late-inspiral and ringdown phases. The authors suggest that this could be a definitive test of whether black holes are truly “macroscopic quantum states” or purely classical entities. The caveat remains the extreme difficulty of disentangling these microstate corrections from higher-order post-Newtonian effects in the standard waveform templates.

Wednesday, July 1, 2026

arXiv:2607.01174 §

The cleanest of them all: NLO electroweak corrections to vector-boson scattering into doubly polarised ZZ pairs at the LHC

Ansgar Denner et al.

Denner et al. report the first NLO electroweak (EW) corrections to the fully leptonic \(ZZ\) vector-boson scattering (VBS) channel. Using the double-pole approximation to preserve gauge invariance, the authors separate polarization states at the amplitude level and include factorisable EW corrections to both production and decay. They demonstrate that these corrections are substantial, reducing integrated cross sections by approximately 18% (–14% for longitudinal-longitudinal pairs, –18% for transverse-transverse).

This work is critical for the High-Luminosity LHC era, where VBS observables are expected to test electroweak symmetry breaking with high precision. By providing NLO EW control over polarization-sensitive observables—specifically transverse-momentum and angular distributions—the authors enable experimentalists to move beyond simple unpolarized cross-section measurements. The analysis reveals that while EW corrections are significant, they are relatively flat in many angular observables, reinforcing the utility of polarization fractions as a BSM-sensitive probe. A notable caveat is that this precision currently applies to the four-lepton channel; given the low cross-section, meaningful differential measurement will require merging multiple decay channels to overcome statistical limitations.

arXiv:2607.00439 §

Sub-eikonal stress and model dependence of the small-x gluon D-term

Lei Wang

Wang demonstrates that the gluon D-term cannot be extracted from leading-eikonal saturation models (like the standard dipole \(S_x\) or saturation profiles \(Q_s\)). The headline finding is a “no-go” result: the D-term is fundamentally a sub-eikonal stress probe—projected by the symmetric-traceless transverse stress \(T_g^{ij}\)—and therefore cannot be determined by the leading-eikonal momentum form factor \(A_g(t)\).

This provides a necessary theoretical correction for the EIC era, effectively de-coupling spatial tomography from simple eikonal assumptions. By constructing a gauge-invariant “stress-decorated” Wilson line operator, the paper establishes that the sign and magnitude of the gluon D-term depend on sub-eikonal target fields (e.g., \(F^{i-}\)). While the authors show that a specific “anti-aligned” response model leads to a stable negative forward D-term, the broader conclusion is that the D-term’s model dependence is a feature, not a bug, of higher-order gluon dynamics. The reliance on this specific response model remains a theoretical assumption, highlighting that current saturation models remain incomplete for full 3D gluon imaging.

arXiv:2607.01116 §

Inclusive B̅_s ↦X_s̅c ℓν̅ decays from lattice QCD: computational strategy and a first physical result

Alessandro De Santis et al.

De Santis et al. introduce a first-principles lattice QCD framework to calculate the inclusive decay rate of \(\bar B_s \to X_{\bar sc} \ell \bar \nu\). The methodology bridges the gap between lattice data (computed at unphysical, lighter heavy-meson masses up to 4.3 GeV) and the Operator Product Expansion (OPE), which is valid in the heavy-quark limit. By employing a new technique for four-point correlator computation, they achieve a 7% total error on the inclusive decay rate using \(n_f=2+1+1\) ETMC ensembles.

This result is a milestone for flavor physics, as it brings inclusive \(B\)-decays into the domain of non-perturbative lattice calculations, offering a path to resolve long-standing tensions in the flavor sector that have previously relied heavily on phenomenological inputs. The 7% error is dominated by the limited size of the current configuration set, though the methodology is explicitly designed for systematic reduction as larger datasets become available. The primary caveat is the extrapolation to the physical heavy-quark mass limit, which relies on the OPE remaining robust in the region where lattice data can currently be reliably produced.
